# Rev A bring-up log

The as-built rev A, measured against [`../BRINGUP.md`](../BRINGUP.md)'s pass conditions.  Every number here is a reading
in `data/board_<X>/<date>.jsonl` (the time, the instrument and its settings are there); this page is the summary, written
as the stages go.  What a rev B should change is in [`REVB.md`](REVB.md).

**The boards.**  JLCPCB order 13544840A_Y21, delivered 2026-10-28: five boards, two assembled on the top.  The assembled
two are **A** and **B** (marked on the back silk by hand, beside J302); the three bare ones C-E.

| board | J302 soldered | shunts | stage reached | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | | | 0 | |
| B | | | 0 | |

**The software on the bench (2026-10-29).**
- **The PYNQ.** The stock `pynq_z2_v3.0.1.img` (PynqLinux 3.0 "Belfast", kernel `5.15.19-xilinx-v2022.1`, pynq 3.0.1,
  Python 3.10.4). On the lab LAN at 192.168.8.142 by DHCP, and at its static 192.168.2.99. The bench PC's key is
  installed for `xilinx`.
- **The overlay**, `build/pynq/overlay_2022.1/` (from 12:20): Vivado 2022.1, the release PYNQ 3.0 is built on, from the
  working tree at `9561e5d` plus the day's gateware fix (below).
  - Bit sha256 `a416f915...`, `.hwh` `fa0175a7...`.  Timing: WNS +1.298 ns, WHS +0.030 ns.  5023 LUTs, 5511 flip-flops,
    9 BRAM tiles; the core at 0x43C00000 (4 KB).  I2C SCL / SDA each an IBUF + OBUFT, TX_Q1 an OBUFT (build.tcl checks).
  - Deployed to `~/costaslink/` on the PYNQ, where the driver runs under its Python 3.10.
  - Before it, kept for the record and not to be used: `overlay_2022.1_r0` (`b6a5a083...`, the committed gateware)
    and `_r1` (`812aba1f...`, plus the I2C timeout).  Both, like the 2024.1 overlay of 2026-10-11 in
    `build/pynq/overlay/` (`8532eddf...`), drive the I2C lines instead of releasing them.
- **The gateware fix (2026-10-29).**  Vivado synthesized the block design's module reference out of context and
  "Converted tricell instance to logic" (Synth 8-5799, a critical warning in every build so far) on i2c_scl, i2c_sda
  and tx_q1: no I2C transaction could ever have worked on the PYNQ.  `build.tcl` now synthesizes the block design
  globally and refuses a bitstream whose three tristate pins do not end in a tristate buffer.  And the I2C master
  gives a stuck bus up after 10 ms (it waited for SCL forever), with I2C_CTRL [19] timeout, [20] SCL, [21] SDA:
  `tb_costaslink.v` holds SCL low and checks both, `make sim-driver` 27 tests pass.
- **Why these overlays fit the board.** The header's pin map (`gen/pins.py`, the XDC's one source) is unchanged since
  Prompt 17; the RTL's other changes since 2026-10-11 are two renames.
- **The driver:** the repository at `70f9e05` plus the bring-up's changes below.

**On the PYNQ (2026-10-29 11:43):** the board powered with the PYNQ up (its base overlay loaded, the header undriven),
then the overlay loaded by starting `pynq_serve.sh` by hand (sudo, nohup; not yet the boot service).  The supply at
5.30 V from 11:47 (+5V_RF 4.976 V by the board's own V5_MON at 0.93 A).  `stages.watch` guards it while unattended:
off on a current outside 0.6-1.2 A, a trip, +5V_RF outside 4.75-5.30 V, the NTC over 85 C, or the PYNQ silent.  At
12:16:24 it switched the board off by mistake: the server's restart for the new overlay broke its connection, it did
not reconnect and counted the PYNQ silent (fixed: it reconnects once before counting a miss).  The board was off 90 s,
mid stage 1.5 (the EEPROM writes were simply redone), and the PYNQ drove its pins meanwhile.

**The instruments on the bench (2026-10-29)**, all answering through `bench.py check`:

| instrument | type | link | which one |
|---|---|---|---|
| Agilent E3646A | power supply (2 outputs; output 1 to the board) | RS-232, FTDI `A9PUYBUD` | the second unit: `CAL:COUNt?` +61, factory cal 29 Sep 2007, both outputs work (the first unit, +21, has a dead output 1: lab-instrument-control `hp/hp.md`) |
| Fluke 45 | multimeter | RS-232, FTDI `A95AME9U` | serial 9166018 |
| Rigol DSA875 | spectrum analyzer (9 kHz-7.5 GHz) | LAN 192.168.8.114 (raw socket 5555) and USB | DSA8E163700011 |
| Siglent SDS2104X Plus | oscilloscope | LAN 192.168.8.203 and USB | SDS2PDDX6R0722, firmware 5.0.1.3.9R12 |
| Siglent SDG1032X | function generator | LAN 192.168.8.246 and USB | SDG1XCAC3R0195, firmware 1.01.01.30R1B2 |
| Ettus USRP B210 | SDR (50 MHz-6 GHz, 2 TX, 2 RX) | USB 3 | serial 310BR0607, rev 4, firmware 8.0, FPGA 16.0 (UHD 4.9) |
| PYNQ-Z2 | the host | LAN 192.168.8.142; JTAG and console on USB (`usb-Xilinx_TUL_1234-tul-if01`: the console) | |

The scope, the generator and the analyser are used over the LAN for now. Their USB links need lab-instrument-control's
udev rule (`99-bench-instruments.rules`), which the reinstalled PC does not have yet.

## What runs on the board: every firmware and server version

Kept as they change (Jason, 2026-10-31: log the changes made to the gateware and firmware).  The PYNQ's own image
(PYNQ 3.0.1) is unchanged throughout.  Each overlay lives in its own `~/costaslink_<name>/` on the PYNQ with the
driver files beside it; since 2026-10-31 12:55 the boot service `costaslink-netbus` runs the server from the symlink
`~/costaslink_current` (now `~/costaslink_id13/`), and since 2026-11-01 10:31 PST it starts after the image's
`bootpy.service` (whose `/boot/boot.py` loads the base overlay at every boot: 4.27).  The firmware's ID register says
which overlay is loaded.  Since 2026-11-01 10:20 PST the PYNQ is powered from the E3646A's output 2 (12.0 V, 1.5 A
limit, OVP 13.5 V) beside the board on output 1, so the PC can power-cycle it (`bench.py psu cycle`, 4.27).

## Before power (2026-10-28)

Read before any board was powered; both are driver fixes, made and tested in the RTL simulation:

1. **Stage 0.4 / 1.5's "every DAC channel to mid-scale" would have put the modulator's baseband pins at their absolute
   maximum.**  The MCP4728s leave the factory with 0 V on every channel (its Table 4-2).  `bringup_stage1(eeprom=True)`
   wrote 1.65 V to all eight, VR_I and VR_Q included, as power-up defaults.  With the reference at 1.65 V through R203's
   300 ohm, the I ladder's 11 point (the gateware's idle, TX_IDLE = 0x7) sits at (2 x 3.3 / 2.2 k + 1.65 / 300) / (2 / 2.2 k
   + 1 / 300) = 2.0 V on IBBP, and the Q ladder (348 ohm) with TRIG IN high at 2.2 V on QBBP: the ADL5375's absolute maximum
   is 0 to 2 V on those pins (its Table 2), the operating range 0-1 V.  And the driver's DAC shadows started at mid-scale,
   so any later single-channel write (`dac(DAC1, VC_OFF=...)`, stage 2.2's VCXO sweep) would have written the same 1.65 V
   to both references.  Now: `DAC_DEFAULT_V` (VR_I 0.2125 V, VR_Q 0.2456 V, the rest 1.65 V) is the shadows' start, the
   runtime write and the EEPROM default; `dac()` and `dac_eeprom()` refuse a reference over 0.6 V (`VR_MAX_V`, 1.18 / 1.47 V
   at the pins at worst).  BRINGUP.md stage 0.4's sentence wants the same correction.
2. **The LO MON jacks are dark after `pll_init()`.**  J109 LO RX MON (and J204 LO TX MON) take RFOUTB, and the power-up
   words (`max2871_words.vh`) leave RFB_EN at 0.  `lo_mon(which, on)` resends the six words with it set; `pll_tune` keeps
   the setting.  Stage 2.2 calls it.
3. Stage 1.5 now also writes the four window thresholds above the idle (V_MID + 0.1 V: the AD5696R starts at 1.25 V, under
   the arms, which holds its comparators high), and reads the detectors (V5_MON, TEMP) for the pass condition.

| the PYNQ | the PYNQ-Z2 and its 12 V supply (the board does not take the PYNQ's 5 V: JP301 open); a micro-USB cable (its serial console); an Ethernet cable; a microSD card of 16 GB or more and a USB card reader (this PC shows no reader); the shared standoff | 1.5 on |

| a reference | the **SDG1032X** (25 MHz, 1 V p-p open circuit into REF IN J203, which is high impedance: R214 is DNP), a BNC-to-SMA lead | 2.4 |
| heat | the IR camera or thermometer | 1.4 |

The carrier follows (232 x; -1.15 ppm on the analyser's timebase), broadened by the generator's phase noise x 232 (-3 to -7 dBm in 30 Hz).  **The +-25 MHz sidebands follow the generator, not the TCXO**: 10 kHz off, -45 / -48 dBc at the generator's offset, nothing at the TCXO's (-58 to -65, the skirt): the coupling is in the reference's path to U203 (JP201.2 -> C222 -> REF_IN), 10 dB weaker for the generator's 1 V sine than the TCXO's 3.3 V (REVB row 5).  **And linear in the drive** (15:42, `ref_in_amplitude`): 0.75 / 1.0 / 1.4 / 2.0 / 2.8 V p-p at 25.010 MHz raise both sidebands by +2.3-2.8 / +5.2-5.9 / +8.5-9.1 / +11.5-12.5 dB against 20 log of the ratio's +2.5 / +5.4 / +8.5 / +11.4, and +-3f stays at the floor (-74 to -79 dBc): the trace's voltage coupling, not U203's reference buffer (which would square the sine and hold the level).  The TCXO's -36 / -39 dBc against 2.8 V p-p's -36 / -39: its 3.3 V CMOS fundamental (4.2 V p-p of sine at full swing, 3.4 at the datasheet's 90 %) within 2-3 dB.  JP201 back to TCXO after | |

## Stage 3: loopback (board A, the night of 2026-10-29)

The bench Jason left at 16:50: TX ANT -> 10 cm RG-316 -> two 20 dB pads (40.2 dB with the lead) -> RX ANT; the DSA875 on
RX MON (J102); the scope's ch1 REF OUT, ch2 I MON, ch3 Q MON, ch4 VC MON (1 Mohm; checked 16:12: `cabling_check`,
ch2 is I by I_OFF_F); 50 ohm on TX MON's pigtail; JP201 on TCXO.  `bringup/overnight.py` runs the sections; its files
are in `data/board_A/overnight/`, the plots in `data/board_A/plots/` (`plot_board.py`).  The DSA is never set below
the floor that keeps the ADL5380's input at 0 dBm or less (its maximum +15).

The ring (the fast path with a permutation of the states, no fixed point) gives the precise figure: A3r's four states in Gray order 23.54 ns a hop over 502 hops (5.0 GHz) and 24.30 over 822 (5.2); the flip-and-null pair A1q2 24.04-25.35 over 787-830. CMP_I is left out of every count (mask 0x1E): at the inverted lock it sits on its threshold and chatters, and the first pass (`turn_5000_20261029_215447.json`) counted its edges as arrivals. Not timing: vetoed at every confirm delay from 60 to 320 ns (A2 confirmed to 160 ns, then the event's 200 ns end).  V_T2 had been left at V_MID, so the slow Q window read the Q legs' offsets: from 1.85-2.3 V both CMP_QP_S and CMP_QN_S are quiet at the idle (the x20 slow Q stage, U502, holds about 0.1-0.2 V of the arm's static error), yet A1 still vetoed.  The capture (1.25 ns) shows why: through the flip the fast vector goes IP to IN cleanly and CMP_I_S follows, but **CMP_QP_S fires and stays: the flipped point carries a Q of 0.34-0.62 a** (the scope's constellations at DSA 14-29: the flip 146-164 degrees from the idle, not 180).  That Q scales with the signal (0.120 V at a = 0.195, 0.059 at 0.121, 0.021-0.029 at 0.046-0.062), so it is in the TX's output, not a leak after the DSA: the OOK null sits at 0.18-0.22 a, **the modulator's carrier feedthrough at about -14 dBc at 5.0 GHz**, and a constant c added to +-g turns the flip by 2 atan(c / g).  Remedies, none hardware: null the carrier at the working frequency (the ladder's (0, 0) held, the VR / offset trims), or let A1's slow expectation care about CMP_I_S only (the gateware's care bit is one for all three).  The first try at the held points (`flip_points_*`) is not valid (the held loop walks a radian in 50 ms; noted in the log) | |

The pull-in's one-sidedness at 5.0 GHz is an offset push, not the loop's dynamics.**  (Corrected by 4.12: the sequencer's release muted the TX, so the rates below are the integrator against ERR with no carrier, and the 'reversal' at the null is CMP_I chattering on noise.  With the carrier on the push does not depend on I_OFF_F; it is ERR's mid-point under V_MID, and 4.12 maps the unlocked loop's rate against the offset.)  VC held at an offset (TX on, the integrator centred), released 3 ms by the sequencer: with I_OFF_F at V_MID **VC falls at a constant 4.0-4.1 V/s from every offset, -60 to +60 kHz** (CMP_I's duty 0.56-0.57 with the beat, not 0.50: the fast I comparator's offset, REVB 15): toward the lock from above, away from below, so only starts from above lock.  With I_OFF_F at its null (2.96 V, duty 0.49-0.51) the push reverses and grows: +11 to +43 V/s from below (rising with the offset), +43 to +45 V/s from above; releases from -15 to -60 kHz then end in false locks, from +15 to +60 kHz in locks.  The simple model (the push = (2D - 1) times the Q leg's DC error) does not explain the second case; the two mid-points (below) are the next suspect.  **B. (Corrected by 3.8a: these were lock losses, not the eye.)  100 Mbaud read 0.12-0.15 at every sampling phase** (all 8 samples of the 10 ns symbol); 50 Mbaud is error-free at 13 of 16 phases (0, 1 and 9 bad; 7 and 15 at 3 x 10^-4): the end-to-end response needs more than 10 ns to settle, as the turn sweep's 10-13 ns edge (3.4i) says.  **C. FSK works both ways: the earlier 'same order' was the test code** (`linktests.fsk` rewrote TX_POINTS to one point with hold_point(1, 0) after each run, so every run after the first rotated a corrupted table; fixed).  With the table rewritten: forwards and backwards read opposite orders at 1-12.5 MHz (5.2 GHz: 408/409, 818/820, 2047/2048 steps the right way; 5.0 GHz backwards 75-94 %).  The ladder's read-back shows the stray steps' source: two-bit transitions pass through an intermediate code (5 or 15) for 1.25 ns or more, the pins' skew (REVB 16).  **D. The TX carrier nulled at 5.0 GHz with VR_I / VR_Q: -17.6 dBc before, -45.4 dBc at VR_I 0.2527 / VR_Q 0.2757 V** (the trims restored after, 0.2127 / 0.2457).  The A1 flip then sits at 187 degrees with no Q (0.01 a), **but A1 is still vetoed (4000 of 4000)**.  With A1 flipping half the time (2 us events), CMP_QP_S is quiet only with V_T2 at 2.4 V or more (at the idle alone, 1.7 V or more): the x20 slow Q stage's leg rises 0.3-0.8 V at the flip.  Suspect: the loop nulls ERR (one Q leg) against V_MID (R128 / R129, C131), while each THS4541 arm sets its own common mode (Vocm on C121 / C124 / C501 / C506, its internal divider): any difference is a static Q error that changes sign with the symbol and is amplified x20; to check with the Fluke at the next pop-in.  `link/diag_5000_*`, `slowq_5000_*`, `fsk0_*` | |
| 3.7 the pop-in (Jason, 10:15-10:33): test B, the Fluke checks, test C, the loopback back (`podtests.py leak`, `lomon`; `pod/leak_20261030_102155.json`, `pod/lomon_20261030_103106.json`) | test B: the leak falls 20 dB or more without the lead, or it is on the board; test C: sim/aslaid's peak near 3.8 GHz and null near 4.3; the LNA's VCC and V_ENABLE 3.25-3.30 V | **Test B: the leak is on the board.**  TX ANT (J202) and RX ANT (J101) each on a 50 ohm terminator, no lead: the TX carrier at RX MON with the TX on, DSA 31.75, is -53 to -60 dBm over 4.9-5.75 GHz and -44 to -49 at 5.85-6.0, **within -6.5 to +14 dB of the loopback's level** at the same DSA (the 05:53 map), never 20 dB under.  At DSA 0 it reads 5-14 dB away from DSA 31.75, either way: part enters before the DSA and part after, adding as vectors.  Muted, -59 to -88 dBm (the disabled modulator's LO); the RX LO's own leak at RX MON about -100 dBm.  The guard (RX MON at 5.0 GHz, DSA 20) read -39.3 dBm in loopback and -54.8 terminated.  **The Fluke 45** (Jason, black on an SMA shell): V_ENABLE at R101's U101 end 3.033 V, so R101 drops 0.237 V = **2.37 mA, the SKY65404's 2.3 mA typical (high 2.5-3.5 V): the LNA is enabled**; VCC at L102 3.270 V; **V_MID (C131) 1.6328 V, the I arm's Vocm (C121) 1.6191 V, the Q arm's (C124) 1.6193 V: V_MID is 13.7 mV above both arms' common mode** (REVB 17: at the lock each Q leg carries about 13.7 mV that flips with the symbol, x20 in the slow Q stage = about 0.55 V a leg, the 0.3-0.8 V seen in 3.6d).  **Test C: LO RX MON (J109) peaks at -0.6 dBm at 3.80 GHz and dips to -5.8 dBm at 4.30 GHz, as the as-laid model put them** (the null a 5 dB dip, not deep); -7 to -10 dBm over 4.8-6.0 GHz (-8.7 at 5.8, as stage 2.2), -17 to -23 under 3.4 GHz.  The loopback back (10:33): RX MON -39.8 dBm at the guard's setting, locked at 5.0 GHz DSA 20 and 5.2 GHz DSA 10 (VC 1.863 V, CMP_I steady).  `plots/pod_tests_bc.png` | |
| 3.8 the characterisation campaign (`campaign.py`, Jason 10:40: "as wide a range of situations as the board allows"), 10:47-11:21 | | Seven sweeps in loopback at the designed lock; the conclusions here, the numbers in `campaign/*.json`. |
| 3.8a the BER, first pass (`campaign.py ber`) | | **Every non-zero BER today was the loop out of lock, not bit errors.**  The self-synchronising PRBS15 check reads 1/3 for an inverted or constant stream (every check fails) and exactly 1/6 for data times a slow +-1 (the loop beating: half the checks fail); the readings were 0, 1/6 or 1/3, and many exactly 1/6 for a whole second.  **With the loop locked, 1 s runs were error-free at every rate from 10 to 100 Mbaud** (10^7 to 10^8 bits) at 5.0 and 5.2 GHz, 100 Mbaud included (at sampling phase 4).  This corrects 3.6d B: 100 Mbaud is not closed at every phase; this morning's 0.12-0.15 there was the loop slipping and beating.  The phase scans' 'open' counts are not the eye: lock losses during them make phases look bad.  Against the carrier at the regulated DSA: error-free at 3.5, 4.5 (5 x 10^-4 at 50 Mbaud), 4.8-5.4, 5.7 and 5.9 GHz; 5.5-5.6 partly; no lock at 4.0; at 5.8 GHz no signal (the chain, REVB 8) | |
| 3.8b the plan's schemes with PRBS-chosen messages (`campaign.py schemes`: 5.0 and 5.2 GHz, six levels each, windows 0.3-0.6 a, 20 000 events a run) | | **A3 (two bits) decides every event, none wrong or false, over 15 dB of level at each carrier with the windows at 0.3-0.5 a** (legs 56-249 mV at 5.0 GHz, 75-453 mV at 5.2); mean 67.2-70.8 ns, 2.3 ns faster per 0.2 a lower; at 0.6 a 0.5-20 % undecided.  A2: every decided message right, but only 75 % decided, **a gateware bug**: `tx_engine.v`'s `modn` reduces a random 0-7 with two subtractions, enough for N >= 3, so for N = 2 the values 6 and 7 become messages 2 and 3, points nobody defined (events with a PRBS source only; the BER's PRBS symbols take log2(N) bits and are right).  A1 and A1w need the windows at 0.5 a or more: the unnulled carrier feedthrough puts the flip at 0.3-0.6 a of Q, so a 0.3-0.4 a window fires on it.  No idle edges at any setting | |
| 3.8c the event rate (`campaign.py rate`) | | **A1 and A3 take an event every 24 clocks (240 ns, 4.2 M events a second; A3's 8.3 Mbit/s) without an error** (at most 0.02 % undecided); at every 16 clocks the receiver takes every second event (the decoder's re-arm).  Long events (5 us) make false and missed events | |
| 3.8d the fast path's rings at this lock (`campaign.py rings`) | LATENCY.md check a | The axis-point ring 22-31.6 ns a hop (fastest at 0.4 a, 22-24 ns), the +-I flip 21.5-27.4 ns (rising about 2.5 ns from 0.2 to 0.7 a: the next state's window fires further along the swing); level barely matters.  A3's corner ring did not start in this frame (0 hops: its table, not looked into).  The fast path stays 22-27 ns RX ANT to TX ANT, 1-3 ns over last night's best at the inverted lock | |
| 3.8e the per-carrier tables (`campaign.py cal`) | | **The TX carrier nulls at VR_I 0.238-0.260 V, VR_Q 0.273-0.288 V everywhere from 4.9 to 5.6 GHz (-50 to -58 dBc); the trims as set (0.2127 / 0.2457) leave -17 to -19 dBc across the whole band**: they are about 40 mV off, from a 5.8 GHz null the dead chain there confounded.  One setting near 0.250 / 0.280 V would hold about -45 dBc or better over 4.9-5.6 GHz (Jason's call: the EEPROM defaults).  The fast I comparator's null I_OFF_F 2.70-3.01 V over the band (REVB 15) | |
| 3.8f the loop against the level (`campaign.py dyn`, 5.0 GHz) | f_n and damping as the square root of the leg | f_n 3.5-4.0 kHz at a 173 mV leg, 2.5 at 95, 2.2 at 69, 2.0 at 55 mV; damping 0.6-0.9 down to 0.34-0.51: as the square root of the leg (x1.8 against x1.9).  An unregulated level moves the loop's bandwidth and damping by 2x over 10 dB | |
| 3.8g the slow path's working point, the carrier nulled (`campaign.py slow`) | | **With the carrier nulled, A1, A2 and A3 are all confirmed at V_T2 2.7-3.1 V** (A1 fully from 2.5 V at 5.0 GHz, 2.7 at 5.2); under 2.3 V A1 is vetoed (REVB 17's 13.7 mV, x20).  V_T2 = 2.9 V is the setting | |
| 3.8h the BER in segments (`campaign.py ber2`: runs of 20-40 ms classed clean / inverted / random / partial; the data's push on the lock) | | **The eye at a fixed sampling phase, not the loop, limits the BER; and reading 1/6 means random bits, which a bad phase gives as surely as a beating loop.**  At 5.0 GHz DSA 20: 100 Mbaud clean at 2 of 8 phases (3-4: about 2.5 ns of the 10 ns symbol; 0 errors in 1.6 x 10^7 bits a phase, 0 in 10^8 at DSA 16-20), random at 0, 6, 7, partial at 1, 2, 5; 50 Mbaud clean at 11 of 16; 25 and 10 Mbaud nearly every phase.  Each phase behaves the same in all its segments, and the lock was checked after, so these are the eye.  **The eye moves with the level**: at a fixed phase 100 Mbaud is clean only over DSA 16-20 at 5.0 GHz (DSA 4-6 and 10-12 at 5.2, errors at 8): a fixed-phase BER against the DSA is the eye's drift, not the sensitivity; a synchronous data mode would need symbol timing recovery (the event decoder, deciding on the first edges, does not).  **The data's push on the lock is benign**: with PRBS at 1-100 Mbaud the Q leg sits 24-43 mV off its idle level whatever the rate and VC does not move (the unnulled carrier feedthrough averaged over +-a, not the loop switch's charge injection, which would grow with the rate); the lock held after every rate.  To look into: at 50 Mbaud the bad phases recur every 10 ns clock (samples 0-1 and 8-9), not only at the symbol boundary | |
| 3.8i the fabric's clock changed (`fclk_tests.py`: FCLK0 on the PYNQ through `pynq_fclk.py`, the clock wizard reset to relock; the watchdog paused; 11:30-11:57) | Jason: "Can you alter the FPGA PLLs to test things at rates other than 50 and 100 MHz?" | **The board runs at 76.9, 83.3, 90.9, 100 and 111.1 MHz** (FCLK0 1000 MHz / 13, 12, 11, 10, 9; the MMCM's VCO 615-889 MHz), ID, I2C and both synthesizers checked at each, 100 MHz restored and checked after.  **BPSK at one symbol a clock is error-free at 76.9, 83.3, 90.9 and 100 Mbaud** (7.7 x 10^7 to 10^8 bits each; 4-5 of 8 phases open at 76.9-90.9, 2 at 100) and at half those; **111.1 Mbaud has real bit errors (BER 0.02-0.12, at most 1 phase partly open)**: the end-to-end edge's limit lies between 100 and 111 Mbaud, about 9-10 ns a symbol (111 MHz is outside the build's timing closure, flagged).  A1 and A3 decide every event at every clock.  **The ring is the same at every clock** (the +-I ring 25.7-26.3 ns a hop at 83.3, 100 and 111.1 MHz; the axis ring 23.5-25.5 ns): the fast path is combinational and the samples' times are right.  **But the decision latency falls as the clock slows**: A3's mean 72.8 ns at 111 MHz, 70.0 at 100, 67.8 at 90.9, 66.1 at 83.3, 64.3 at 76.9 (A1 the same way, with 2-4 ns of run-to-run spread): about -2.1 ns per ns of clock period, which a causal pipeline cannot give.  The TX-event reference must move with the clock relative to the pins: not understood; to read in rx_engine.v / tx_engine.v before trusting the absolute decision latency or LATENCY.md's clocked rows | |
| 3.9 the lock and its loss, for the link's real conditions (`locklab.py`; Jason 11:15: "loss of lock will continue to be the biggest problem ... interference and a low and changing signal amplitude"), the TX carrier nulled per carrier from 3.8e's table during each test, the trims restored after | | |
| 3.9a what tells the lock (`locklab.py detect`: 5.0 GHz at DSA 20 / 26 / 29, 5.2 GHz at DSA 10 / 16 / 19; locked, held off the lock by 2-50 kHz, the TX muted, far releases) | | **CMP_I's edges in a 10 ms gate separate locked from unlocked at every level: 0 locked, 1 100-19 000 beating or false-locked.**  With no signal CMP_I is quiet too, so the second test is the I leg away from its no-carrier level (or an IP / IN window steadily high).  The windows: 0 edges locked, 3 500-46 000 beating.  LOG_Q 1.12-1.51 V locked against 1.56-2.20 beating and 1.04-1.28 with no signal: it follows the level as well as the lock, so only with CMP_I.  **A false lock is a beat**: VC parked at 1.705 V at 5.0 GHz (about 61 kHz off) or 1.72-1.75 / 1.95 V at 5.2, with CMP_I's edges and LOG_Q a beat's; the same detector catches it.  False locks come from releases 30-60 kHz below the lock (5.0 GHz, and 5.2 at DSA 16), from both sides at 5.2 GHz DSA 10: a stronger signal, more of them.  A gateware lock detector needs nothing new: the gate's CMP_I count and the window states, every millisecond | |
| 3.9b dropouts (`locklab.py drop`: the TX muted by the sequencer for 1 us - 1 s, the loop free or held through it, five each, at two levels a carrier) | | **Holding the loop through a gap prevents every loss, up to 1 s; a gap of 10 ms or more then lands on either Costas point (20-100 % slipped by 180 degrees).**  Free: back on the same side after gaps up to 1 ms; 10 ms mixed; from 30 ms lost every time (the free integrator walks off; at DSA 26 from 10 ms), except at 5.2 GHz DSA 10, where the disabled modulator's LO leak is strong enough at RX to keep the loop locked through a 1 s gap.  Held: never lost; the slips from 10 ms on mean the phase walks a quarter turn in about 10 ms while held, which needs tens of Hz of frequency error, far more than the drift of a hold (3.6a: about a radian a second); suspect the hold switch's charge step into C130 (a few pC into 100 nF is about 20 Hz at RF).  **Conclusion: blank (hold) the loop through any gap or burst, and carry the 180-degree ambiguity in the data** (differential encoding, or a reference symbol after a gap) | |
| 3.9c fades (`locklab.py fade`: the DSA stepped 3-25 dB for 10 ms, 0.1 s, 1 s and back, three each) | | **All 108 fades held, none slipped**, at 5.0 and 5.2 GHz, down to DSA 31.75 (a 44 mV leg; at 5.2 GHz below where the loop can acquire, DSA 28+).  Once locked the loop rides through deep fades; the DSA's own switching makes no slip.  Acquisition, not hold-in, is the weak-signal limit | |
| 3.9d an interferer emulated on the board (`locklab.py cw`, stopped) | | **Not a valid test of interference, logged so it is not reused.**  The 'pilot' was meant as a weak carrier from VR_I / VR_Q with the ladder at (0, 0) and the 'interferer' the ladder rotating at 10 kHz-12.5 MHz; but the lock's leg was 0.23-0.26 V whatever the pilot's size (hold_point() sends its message as set, the full idle here), and the rotation then replaced the carrier, so each run was a 1 s dropout with an offset tone, lost as 3.9b's free loop.  An interferer needs a second, independent source: the USRP B210 over the air (b210.py), next | |
| 3.9e the hold's phase memory (`locklab.py holdwalk`: the I/Q phasor on the scope after LOOP_HOLD, the signal on; `drop2`: held TX_DIS gaps of 1 ms - 0.3 s with the hold kept 0, 20 us, 200 us, 2 ms after the TX returns, six each) | | **The hold keeps the phase: the held phasor walks 0.01-2.5 Hz (eight holds at 5.0 and 5.2 GHz), a few hundredths of a radian over 70 ms.  Yet held TX_DIS gaps of 10 ms or more still come back slipped 30-60 % whatever the guard.**  So the receiver's hold is not what slips, nor the moment of release.  The modulator cannot restart at 180 degrees (the ADL5375's LO is a polyphase splitter, no divider; DSOP gates the output stage, 220 ns on).  The contrast with 3.9c is the evidence: a 25 dB DSA fade for 1 s never slipped, a TX_DIS gap of 10 ms slips half the time, and TX_DIS switches 76 mA of the modulator's supply (203 / 127 mA).  **Likely: the load step pushes a reference (the TCXO or the VCXO, a few ppb: 25 Hz or more at RF) while the TX is off** (not measured directly: nothing to see the phase by while the TX is off).  So 3.9b's slips are largely an artifact of muting our own TX in loopback, not what a fade does; but the plan's own T/R sequencing switches that same load, so a held loop keeps its phase across a TX burst only for about 1 ms | |
| 3.9f recovery (`locklab.py recover`: a VC_OFF kick of +-39-123 kHz for 0.5 s, then VC_OFF back; eight each at two levels a carrier) | | **A lost loop stays lost (beating or railed) after the kick ends; a firmware-style recovery brought it back 96 of 96 times on its first try**: hold, centre the integrator at DSA 31.75 (`loop_center`), release from 10 kHz above the lock (the unlocked push carries it in), check with 3.9a's detector.  6.0-7.4 s each from the PC, nearly all of it the centring's network-timed steps and a 2 s settle; the same sequence in gateware is tens of ms.  At 5.2 GHz DSA 10 a +41 kHz kick was survived 6 of 8 times (2 back on the other point) | |
| 3.9g the weak end over time (`locklab.py slips`: 30 s at each DSA, about 23 looks a second) | | **No slip and no loss in 8.5 minutes and about 12 500 looks at any level where the loop locked**: 5.0 GHz from DSA 20 to 31.75 (a 47 mV leg), 5.2 GHz from DSA 10 to 28 (31 mV); 5.2 GHz at DSA 30-31.75 does not acquire.  In loopback, noise does not break the lock even at the weak end | |
| 3.9h where releases end (`locklab.py falselock`: -60 to +60 kHz in 5 kHz steps, two levels a carrier) | | **The capture window is narrow and lopsided: -10 to +60 kHz at 5.0 GHz DSA 20 and 5.2 GHz DSA 16, +5 to +60 at 5.0 GHz DSA 26, -10 to +20 at 5.2 GHz DSA 10.**  Below it the loop parks in a false lock at a fixed VC (1.70-1.71 V at 5.0 GHz, about -61 kHz; 1.72-1.75 V at 5.2, -46 to -57 kHz) or, at the weaker 5.0 GHz level, runs VC toward its rail; at 5.2 GHz DSA 10 releases from +25 kHz and above park at VC 1.948 V (+36 kHz).  Starting 10-20 kHz above the lock acquires in every case, which is why 3.9f's recovery succeeds at once.  For a link between two boards, whose TCXOs may differ by 1-2 ppm (5-10 kHz at 5 GHz), the first acquisition cannot rely on a single release: it needs a sweep (from above, downward, through VC_OFF) or a frequency-acquisition aid | |
| 3.9i does TX_DIS push the TX's reference? (`link/tx_push_20261030_125047.json`: 5.0 GHz, the carrier with the TX on and the disabled modulator's LO leak with it off, both at RX MON at 10 Hz RBW, four cycles of 20 s each way) | 3.9e's suspicion | **Yes: the TX LO sits 35-80 Hz lower at RF (7-16 ppb) with the TX disabled**, on top of a -100 Hz drift over the two minutes of toggling (the TCXO answering the duty-cycled heat, or the analyser's own timebase).  +5V_RF sags 39 mV with the TX on (4.9685 to 4.9293 V) and the supply current rises 102 mA (0.940 to 1.042 A).  A 40 Hz step turns the phase a quarter turn in about 6 ms: 3.9b and 3.9e's slips after held gaps of 10 ms or more.  The TCXO (or what feeds it) is pushed by the modulator's switched load, through the supply or heat; which one is not separated | |
| 3.9j acquisition from a real board-to-board offset (`locklab.py search2`, 5.0 GHz: the TX synthesizer fractional-N with `max2871_regs.regs_frac`, k x 6.105 kHz off the 25 MHz grid, the RX on it; a downward stepped search from +120 kHz above the nominal lock, 15 kHz a step, 3.9a's detector; also `search`, the TX rotated through its axis points, which a BPSK Costas loop sees as a QPSK-like signal: not a clean offset, set aside) | | **All 26 cases locked on the first release from +120 kHz: 13 offsets from -73 to +73 kHz, at DSA 20 and DSA 26**; each lock's VC agrees with its offset to about 10 % (Kv's calibration).  So the unlocked loop's downward push (3.6d) is an acquisition sweep of its own: from 120 kHz above the nominal it carries the loop down through the true lock up to 190 kHz, before the false-lock attractor below it.  At 5.0 GHz, 'release well above' acquires any offset to +-15 ppm in one try (7 s from the PC, about a 50 ms release in gateware).  **At 5.2 GHz, DSA 16: all 13 on the first release too; at DSA 10 (the strong signal, with 3.9h's false attractor 36 kHz above the lock) all 13 found, in 1-11 tries (typically 7-9): releases far above park in the upper false lock, the detector rejects them, and the search locks from 0-30 kHz above.  So 52 of 52 real offsets from -73 to +73 kHz were acquired by the downward stepped search; in gateware each try is a release and a ~50 ms look, so under a second even at the worst** | |
| 3.9 so far: the conclusions | | **(1) Without interference the lock is solid**: no slips in 8.5 minutes at any lockable level, 25 dB fades for 1 s held, the DSA's switching harmless (3.9c, 3.9g).  **(2) A lost loop never comes back by itself** (it rails or false-locks), **but a simple recovery always does**: detect, hold, centre, release 10-20 kHz above, check (96 of 96, 3.9f).  **(3) The lock is easy to detect** from CMP_I's edges and the windows' states (3.9a).  **(4) Acquisition is the fragile step**: a lopsided capture window (-10 to +20..+60 kHz) with false-lock attractors 36-61 kHz off (3.9h), and weak signals that hold but cannot acquire (3.9c, 3.9g); a link between boards will need a sweep.  **(5) Gaps**: holding through a gap prevents loss; the 180-degree point after a gap of our own TX of 10 ms or more is random, likely the TX's load step pushing a reference (3.9e); the protocol must carry the ambiguity.  **(6) Interference** could not be tested in loopback (3.9d): the B210 over the air is next | |
| 4.0 over the air with the FPV antennas and the USRP B210 (Jason, 13:30-14:50: four antennas within 30-40 cm, some directional; the loopback's lead and pads off; `ota.py`, `b210.py`) | | **The links, after three rearrangements** (`link/ota_links2_20261030_145053.json`): the board's own link locks at 5.2 GHz at DSA 13.75 with a 188 mV leg (about 14 dB of DSA headroom), at 5.0 GHz at DSA 21.75 (156 mV), at 5.5 GHz at DSA 7.75 (160 mV); the B210 hears the board at -13 to -17 dBFS (RX gain 30); **the B210's CW reaches the board's receiver at -23 dB to +11 dB of the wanted carrier** (TX gain 40-89.75, measured at the receiver: the I/Q beat against the locked leg, LOG_Q 24 mV/dB).  RX MON is no measure of the air's signal here: the on-board leak (REVB 10, test B) sits within a few dB of it at DSA 31.75, and the B210's field reaches the analyser's pigtail directly; the I leg and LOG_Q are.  The B210's TX underflowed at 20 Ms/s from Python (gaps in the tone): tones now at 2 Ms/s and bursts at 10 Ms/s with 50 ms buffers | |
| 4.1 the interference ladder at 5.2 GHz (`ota.py interfere`: the loop locked, DSA 13.75; the B210's CW 0.1, 1, 5, 20 MHz off, then 8 MHz noise bursts at 20 / 50 / 100 % duty, its gain stepped 30-89.75, 2 s a step; the lock during (three looks) and after) | Jason: "loss of lock will probably be the way that the interference hurts us" | **In 70 steps the loop was never left lost** (corrected in 4.3: during a CW 5 or 20 MHz above at gain 80 and more it was dragged off, VC at its rail, and came back by itself after).  CW at every offset: CMP_I silent and the loop locked through gain 75 (the interferer about 2 dB under the carrier); at gain 80 (about +3 dB) CMP_I toggles while it is on (the decisions fail) but the loop holds; at 89.75 (about +11 dB) the loop comes back 180 degrees slipped at 0.1, 1 and 20 MHz, locked at 5 MHz.  Noise bursts (8 MHz wide, 0.5 of full scale RMS, so about 3 dB under the CW): clean to gain 70; CMP_I disturbed from 75, the loop locked after at every duty, one slip at 100 % and full gain.  **So up to about +11 dB of interference the decisions fail first (from about 0 dB) and the loop last (a slip, not a loss)**: the loop's 3 kHz bandwidth averages what the arms' 94 MHz lets in.  What to engineer: the decisions' errors under interference (the window thresholds, a confirm, the data's coding) and the 180-degree ambiguity after a strong burst.  The lock detector must not read CMP_I's activity during interference as a loss: LOG_Q and PEAK_Q say interference (2.0-2.4 V against 1.3-1.5 locked and quiet) | |
| 4.2 the link over the air, no interference (`weekend.py baseline`, 15:00: the carrier nulled, the windows at 0.5 a, V_T2 2.9 V) | | **Every scheme decoded at 5.0, 5.2 and 5.5 GHz with nothing wrong, false or missed, and every decided event confirmed by the slow path (no vetoes)**: A1 20 000 / 20 000 at 78.8-81.2 ns (72.5 in loopback: the antennas' leads of a few feet and 30-40 cm of air add about 7 ns), A1w 73.8-76.2, A2 all right (75 % decided: REVB 22), A3 19 869-20 000 right of 20 000 at 71.2-80.0 ns.  BPSK: 100 Mbaud error-free at 5.2 and 5.5 GHz (5 x 10^7 bits each, 2 of 8 phases open), 1 % at 5.0 GHz (the eye's timing at that level, 3.8h); 50 Mbaud and below error-free everywhere.  At 5.5 GHz 4 of 10 segments at 25 Mbaud (and 1 of 10 at 10) came back random with no bit errors: the loop disturbed during data on that carrier (U-NII-2C); the weekend's rounds repeat it | |
| 4.3 round 1 at 5.2 GHz (`weekend.py rounds`, 15:05-15:18: DSA 13.75, leg 181 mV, windows 0.5 a, V_T2 2.9 V, the carrier nulled; the B210's CW 0.1 / 1 / 5 / 20 MHz **above** the carrier and 8 MHz noise at 20 and 100 % duty, gain 0 and 60-89.75; interferer to carrier from 4.0's calibration, about 1 dB a step: -16 dB at gain 60, -6.4 at 70, +3.2 at 80, +11.4 at 89.75) | | **The loop is dragged off the carrier by a strong interferer, which 4.1 missed**: 4.1 and this round find the loop locked *after* every CW, but *during* it the I leg's DC collapsed (-0.006 against -0.18 V) and VC sat at 3.27 V, the VCO's top rail (about +580 kHz): at 5 MHz from gain 80 (+3 dB), at 20 MHz from gain 78 (+1 dB); at 0.1 and 1 MHz only at 89.75 (+11 dB, VC 1.97 and 2.83 V), held to 86 (+9 dB) with CMP_I toggling.  4.1's own data says the same (VC 3.27 V during at 5 and 20 MHz, gain 80 and 89.75).  Every CW came back by itself within 0.3 s of stopping, but every CW was above the carrier: dragged up, the unlocked loop drifts down (REVB 18) and through the capture window.  The near-carrier capture shows the other side: a CW 50 kHz *below* at +11 dB dragged VC 25 kHz down and the loop **stayed lost** after (the drift takes it further down); within +-20 kHz at +3 dB and up the loop follows the CW (VC -6.6 kHz for a CW at -10 kHz, +14.3 kHz for one at +20 kHz at +11 dB: locked to the interferer) and returns to the carrier after, a third of the time slipped.  The 8 MHz noise never dragged it; once (100 % duty, full gain) it slipped during and was found lost after, VC at 0.5 V, the bottom rail.  Why, as far as the data go: sign(I) is a hard limiter; once the interferer is the larger in the I arm sign(I) follows the interferer, the carrier's restoring force averages away (a limiter's capture effect), and the I comparator's delay against the Q path (10-13 ns, REVB 20) turns the beat into a DC term going as sin(2 pi f_b tau): 0.6 % of a cycle at 0.1 MHz, 0.3-1.3 rad at 5-20 MHz, which is why the threshold falls from +9..+11 dB near the carrier to about +1 dB at 20 MHz.  It predicts the drag's sign follows the interferer's side: `pull` (CW 0.1-40 MHz above and below) and `pullhold` (the loop held through the CW: what an interference hold saves) are added to the rounds to test it (REVB 27).  **The decisions** (A3, windows at 0.5 a, 20 000 PRBS events 20 us apart): the first effect is erasure, not error: at -16 dB 9-11 % of the events undecided and none wrong at every offset; misses from about -11 dB (CW 0.1 MHz: 7 %); false events from -6 dB, where the interferer reaches the windows' distance (0.5 a), 0.15-2 million in 0.4 s paced by the beat; and at 20 MHz from -4.5 dB (5 MHz from +6 dB) **no event at all** (every reference missed: the comparators chatter at the beat and the detector, which re-arms after 4 quiet clocks, never re-arms).  Noise bursts (8 MHz, 3 dB under the CW): the first wrong decisions at gain 65 (-11 dB; 31 wrong and 141 false at 20 % duty, 221 and 1 298 at 100 %).  **The slow path vetoes the noise's wrong decisions almost one for one** (20 % duty, gain 72: 68 010 wrong, 67 219 vetoed; 44 801 right, 44 816 confirmed), not a near CW's (0.1-1 MHz: the slow comparators follow the beat and confirm it), and under a 5 MHz CW it vetoes the right ones as well (gain 72: 922 confirmed of 601 961 right): a filter against fast interference, worth having, not a check on its own.  **Pulses** (8 MHz noise 10 us every 1 ms, 100 us every 10 ms, 1 ms every 20 ms; gain 80 and 89.75; three each): locked after all 18; false events in proportion to the time under the bursts (about 3 a microsecond of burst), fewer at full gain than at 80 (the comparators saturate and stop re-arming).  **The BPSK segments at 25 Mbaud** came back random (r 0.1-0.28) in 11 of 30 segments at gain 0, the B210 silent, the loop locked, A3 all right: not interference (4.2's 5.5 GHz segments again); `segcheck` added to find out | |
| 4.3a round 1 at 5.0 and 5.5 GHz (15:18-15:37; 5.0 GHz DSA 21.75, leg 155 mV; 5.5 GHz DSA 7.75, leg 159 mV; the rest as 4.3) | | The same pattern, the thresholds moved by the B210's reach at each carrier (the antennas' responses: the gain steps are not the same ratio at each).  **The drag** (the I leg collapsed, VC at a rail, during the interferer): CW 0.1 / 1 / 5 / 20 MHz above from gain 89.75 / 89.75 / 80 / 78 at 5.2 GHz; - / - / 89.75 / 86 at 5.0 GHz; 86 / 89.75 / 78 / 76 at 5.5 GHz: always the 20 MHz CW first, then the 5 MHz, and the near ones last (4.3's comparator-delay reading).  **Coming back**: at 5.2 GHz every time within 0.3 s; **at 5.0 GHz three times not within 0.3 s** (CW 5 MHz at 89.75, 20 MHz at 86 and 89.75: from the top rail at about -4 V/s, REVB 18, the drift needs about 0.35 s to reach the lock; whether it gets there is `pull`'s question, which polls 3 s); **at 5.5 GHz the 8 MHz noise at 100 % duty dragged the loop to the bottom rail** (VC 0.61 V at gain 86) **and it stayed lost after both strong steps** (the search brought it back): noise centred on the carrier unlocks the loop, and the unlocked push runs it down, the side from which it never returns by itself.  The decisions: false events and wrong decisions from gain 70 (CW) and 65 (noise) at 5.2 and 5.5 GHz, 72-78 at 5.0 GHz | |
| 4.4 the B210 as the link's other end, both ways (15:38-16:20; `b210link.py`; the rounds stopped for it) | Jason: "We'll need timing recovery when we go to two Costas boards, so you might as well start now going from the B210 to the Costas board and also vice versa" | **B210 -> board works, events with no timing recovery at all**: the board's own carrier heard by the B210 at +4 307 Hz in its frame (+0.83 ppm, the two references), so the B210 sent its carrier there (its LO parked 1 MHz off, the board's TX muted) and the board locked to it at DSA 13.75 with a 167 mV leg, VC 0.6 kHz from its own-link lock.  A1 and A3 events from the B210 (every 20 us, 0.5 us long, one message at a time for 0.5 s, the decision 250 ns after the event): **A1 25 169 events, all decided right; A3's four messages about 25 000 each, all decided right, none wrong** (the B210's I / Q sense is the board's own TX's: the same frame).  **BPSK from the B210 at 1 Mbaud through the board's fixed-phase check**: every phase reads clean in 20 ms, but held at one phase 10 of 60 runs of 20 ms carry about 4 % errors: the sampling instant drifting through a symbol's edge at the clocks' offset, the timing recovery's reason (the two "trimmed" runs used a wrong offset and mean nothing).  **Board -> B210 works, error-free from 0.1 to 5 Mbaud, through a software receiver that recovers the carrier and the timing** (`sdr_rx.py`; RX gain 45, the B210's master clock set for even decimations, its analog filter at the rate, every packet's overflow counted): 159 132 / 397 932 / 748 449 / 499 482 bits at 0.1 / 1 / 2.5 / 5 Mbaud with no error, EVM 0.09-0.11; **the PYNQ's clock reads -5.30 ppm against the B210's** (+-0.05 at every rate); the board's carrier +5.2 kHz in the B210's frame.  10 Mbaud needs 40 Ms/s and every such capture overflowed (bell at load 36 on 32 cores; at 20 Ms/s 3 of 4).  **My first attempt read random bits for an error of mine, not the link's**: `from_board` never tuned the synthesizers, left at 5.5 GHz by the stopped rounds, so the B210 at 5.2 GHz heard a spur 44 dB under the TX (a line that followed no ladder point and no VR); tuned, the modulated signal arrives at -1 to -3 dBFS at RX gain 70 at every carrier from 4.9 to 5.9 GHz.  (Jason's questions found it: the antenna input (RF A RX2, UHD channel 0, is right: 16 dB over RF A TX/RX, 30 dB over RF B's 915 MHz antenna), the RX gain (C/N0 best at 70, 67.5 dB-Hz on the spur), the bandwidth, the CPU load, the carrier.) | |
| 4.5 the pull: a strong CW above and below the carrier (`weekend.py` pull and pullhold, 5.2 GHz, 16:32-16:50: DSA 17.75, leg 170 mV, the B210's CW 3 s at +-0.1 to +-40 MHz, gain 74-89.75 (about -3 to +12 dB over the carrier), the lock read three times during and polled 3 s after) | 4.3's prediction: the drag follows the interferer's side | **Confirmed: a CW above drags VC to the top rail (+578 kHz), one below to the bottom rail (-672 kHz).**  From gain 83 (about +6 dB) at +-10, +20 and +40 MHz; 86 at +-5, -20, -40; 89.75 at +-2, -1, -0.3; never at +-0.1 (the loop held, CMP_I beating, from 83).  **After the CW, none of the 24 dragged cases came back within 3 s, either side**: from below VC stays at the bottom (0.23 -> 0.34 V); from above it drifts down from the rail and stops at 1.971 V, **+45 kHz, a false-lock attractor** (REVB 26), beating there.  (Round 1 at DSA 13.75 saw it return to the true lock within 0.3 s at 5.2 GHz: at this level, 4 dB lower, the drift is caught on the way: the return is level-dependent luck, not a property.)  **Held** (`pullhold`: LOOP_HOLD from 0.2 s before the CW to 0.2 s after, at +-0.05, +-1, +-5, +-20 MHz, gain 80, 86, 89.75): **24 of 24 back within 0.05 s of the release, 23 locked and 1 slipped 180 degrees**, VC moving 1-5 kHz while held.  **So the cure is in hand on rev A: hold the loop while an interferer is present.**  It needs a detector fast against the drag (the loop's 3-4 kHz: a few hundred microseconds) and blind to the link's own events: CMP_I or the windows changing outside an expected event, in gateware, holding the loop until they have been quiet a while; and a lock detector with an automatic search for what still gets through (REVB 21, 25, 26) | |
| 4.6 why BPSK segments read random with the loop locked (`weekend.py segcheck` 16:47, then by hand 16:50-16:57; 5.2 GHz over the air, nothing interfering) | round 1: a third of the 25 Mbaud segments at r ~ 1/6 with the B210 silent | **Not the link, and not the loop: the BER check's "phase" is not a phase for symbols of more than one clock.**  The check counts its symbols from the write of RX_BER_CFG and the TX its own from TX_GO, both writes arriving over the network at arbitrary clocks, so for a period of p clocks the sampling point lands at phase + 8 k samples into the TX's symbol, k random in 0..p-1 on every run.  Measured: the lock unchanged before and after every bad segment (I 169 mV, CMP_I silent, VC 1.861), the legs' DC during the data the same in good and bad runs; bad runs at 50 Mbaud only at phases 5-7 and 13-15 (half the runs each), at 25 Mbaud at 7, 14-15, 22-23, 31 (a quarter each): the one edge zone (the 10-13 ns edge, REVB 20) seen through each of the p offsets.  In simulation the same RTL never errs (ideal comparators: no edge zone).  **This rewrites every BER phase scan at 50 Mbaud and below** (the "50 Mbaud bad phases at the 10 ns clock" left open in 3.8, 4.2's random segments at 5.5 GHz, 4.3's, the "beating" class of `segs`): a run is clean or random by the draw of k, and "open N of M phases" counts draws.  100 Mbaud (one clock a symbol) is unaffected in principle.  The cure is gateware: the check's frame started from the TX's symbol strobe in loopback, and from the received edges otherwise, which the timing recovery (ID version 4, building) does | |

| 4.8 the gateware's holds (Jason 18:10: "If someone walks in front of the antenna and blocks it or their phone wifi interferes, the lock should recover once the incoming signal and interference levels recover"; overlay 0x434C0005 `801780a`, Vivado 2022.1, WNS +0.235 ns, deployed 19:24 to `~/costaslink_holds/`; `holds_tests.py holds` 19:26) | each switched and tuned from software, off at reset | **The signal holdover works**: the B210's carrier (the board's TX muted) gone for 0.3 s and for 2 s, as if blocked: held, the loop was locked again at once both times (1 holdover each, 0.30 and 2.01 s held; the 2 s one 180 degrees over); free, lost both times (VC 1.749 V, a false lock; 0.381 V, near the bottom rail: the board's own drift, about 300 kHz/s, carries the free loop off while the carrier is gone).  **The first interference hold did not save the loop, and showed why**: through a 1 s CW at +9 dB 10 MHz above or below, VC stayed within 3 kHz while held (free: to +578 / -672 kHz), but the hold reached its 1 s maximum with the CW still on (the B210's stream starts sending inside its setup, before the test's clock started), was released into it, the loop dragged about 37 kHz, and then the lost loop's own beat made the Q windows chatter and re-held it 86 times, freezing it 37 kHz off.  **Fixed in `ihold.v`**: a hold starts only after a quiet window (an interferer arriving at a locked loop finds it armed, a lost loop never), after a release at the maximum it waits for quiet, and the maximum is 5 s; `tb_ihold.v` adds the cases; rebuilt.  `docs/LOCK.md` written: how the loop holds and loses lock, why a dragged loop stays lost (the lock window is in frequency difference; out there the board's own drift, about -4 to -8 V/s, outweighs the carrier's 1 / df pull), and the holds | |
| 4.8a the armed interference hold (overlay `1283f89`, WNS +0.163 ns, deployed 20:22 to `~/costaslink_holds2/`; `holds_tests.py` 20:24, then by hand) | | **The arming worked and the drag was prevented**: through the 1 s CW at +9 dB VC stayed at the lock, +1.9 / -2.6 kHz (free: +578 / -672 kHz, then the +45 kHz false lock and the bottom rail), and a quiet locked loop never triggered it (2 s, 0 holds).  **But the hold then held itself**: after the CW the windows read 0 changes, yet the hold stayed on until its maximum.  By hand: the CW's end released the hold, the loop pulled its phase back, and the Q leg crossing one window's threshold slowly chattered on noise (the windows have no hysteresis): busy, so held, frozen with Q at the threshold, so busy again.  **Fixed**: a busy window now needs two of the chosen comparators (QP and QN) each active in at least half the threshold: an interferer's beat swings Q across both windows, noise at one threshold chatters one.  The signal holdover again brought the loop back at once after 0.3 s and 2 s of a blocked carrier (free: lost, VC 1.754 and 0.551 V) | |

Half wrong is random bits; the bit count is right for the rate (1.29 M in 50 ms at 25 Mbaud), so the recovery puts out a bit a symbol but samples where the eye is closed.  4.6's frame lottery is gone (the recovery sets the frame), so this is the recovery itself. | 4.10 the weekend at the 20 ms release, and the timing recovery's false lock (rounds restarted 00:49 on `eadd8ae` with the driver copied over, `weekend/rounds6.log`; the eye measured 02:32 on it and 02:36 on overlay 0x434C0006 from `8521517`, Vivado 2022.1, WNS +0.198 ns, WHS +0.035 ns, bit `cbd81bc7...`, deployed to `~/costaslink_cdrpol/`; the rounds restarted 02:39 on it, `weekend/rounds7.log`) | the 20 ms release at three carriers; the random segments at 5.0 GHz explained | **At the 20 ms release, 120 of 120 dragging CWs back in round 1 (40 at each carrier), 160 of 160 with 4.8b's; at 1 ms 154 of 160.**  Nearly every case is now one hold through the whole 3 s CW (3.02 s held), where at 1 ms many were released within milliseconds; the data record `hold_s` 0.02.  No hold under A3, BPSK or quiet at any carrier.  B210 -> board, 1 and 2 Mbaud: 27.1 M bits, no error.  **The random segments at 5.0 GHz are a false lock of the timing recovery, and the reason only 5.0 GHz shows them is the loopback's shared clock.**  The `eye` piece folds CMP_I against the TX's own ladder pins (the capture reads them back) during PRBS: one CMP_I transition per TX transition (1 007 against 1 006: no chatter), the eye open 11-12 of 16 samples at 50 Mbaud and 29 of 32 at 25, and **the falling edges 3.0-3.5 samples (3.8-4.4 ns) after the rising ones at 5.0 GHz, 2.2-2.6 at 5.2 GHz**: duty-cycle distortion, CMP_I's threshold off the I leg's middle on the 10-13 ns edge (REVB 20, 30).  `ber_cdr.v` steered to the mean of both kinds of edge, and with two clusters that loop has a second stable point half a symbol away that samples between them (random bits, the check exactly half wrong; `tb_ber_cdr.v` shows it).  A restart (each write of RX_BER_CFG) starts the frame on a clock boundary, and in loopback the TX's symbols sit at fixed samples of the same clock: so a restart's phase is one of only 2 (50 Mbaud) or 4 (25 Mbaud) values, and whether one of them falls in the false lock's basin depends on where the edges sit within a clock.  The latency is 22 samples at 5.0 GHz and 21 at 5.2: at 5.0 GHz one of the phases falls in the basin (the restarts read back: half a symbol off in 6 of 10 at 50 Mbaud and 3 of 10 at 25, the segments random 4 of 10 and 0 of 10 in this measurement, 7 of 10 and 7 of 40 in the round), at 5.2 GHz none (0 of 60 segments every round).  Against another board's clock the phase is uniform, and the basin (about the skew's width) would catch about a fifth of the restarts at 50 Mbaud at any carrier.  **Cured in gateware (ID version 6): the recovery tracks the rising edges alone (one stable point at any skew), measures the falling edges' mean place against them (RX_CDR_SKEW) and samples half of it later, the middle of the eye** (RX_BER_CFG[29], on by default in the driver).  On the board: 0 of 40 segments random at 5.0 and 5.2 GHz (against 4 of 10 on version 5 the same minutes), no restart half a symbol off, and **RX_CDR_SKEW reads +3.39..+3.43 samples at 5.0 GHz 50 Mbaud, +2.75..+2.83 at 25 Mbaud, +2.4 and +2.06 at 5.2 GHz**, within 0.3 sample of the eye fold's: the gateware now measures CMP_I's duty-cycle distortion in service.  The same build resets RX_IHOLD_HOLD to 20 ms.  `eye` runs in every round from now | |
| 4.11 the first round on overlay 0x434C0006 (`weekend/rounds7.log`, Sat 02:39-05:27 at 5.2, 5.0 and 5.5 GHz; the server's driver and the PC's hash alike, `3261a01f74cd`) | every piece at three carriers on the cured timing recovery | **Nothing lost that a hold could save, and no random segment anywhere.**  `pullauto` (the interference hold on, 20 ms release): **120 of 120 dragging CWs back** (5.2 GHz 34 on the same side + 6 on the other, 5.0 GHz 24 + 16, 5.5 GHz 29 + 11); 119 dragged the I leg below half during the CW, one did not (5.2 GHz, -40 MHz, gain 80); 113 were one hold through the whole 3 s CW, 6 two holds, one three (0.88 s held: that CW dragged the loop only at its first look).  At the 20 ms release now **280 of 280** (4.8b's 40, 4.10's 120, these 120).  A quarter come back on the other side (33 of 120; 19 of 120 in 4.10): the held VC is not exactly the lock, so the phase walks in 3 s; BPSK's 180 degree ambiguity, which the data's framing must resolve anyway.  Held by hand (`pullhold`): 72 of 72.  **Without a hold (`pull`) every loss is below the carrier again**: 19 of 96 lost at 5.2 GHz, 8 at 5.0, 28 at 5.5, and of the CWs that dragged the loop all 43 above came back, none of the 55 below (4.9's mechanism: the free loop's own push is downward, REVB 18).  `capture` (a CW within +-50 kHz, no hold): 5.0 GHz 36 of 36 back, 5.2 GHz 35 of 36, and **at 5.5 GHz the 12 at full gain (89.75) all lost, VC dragged 39-91 kHz down, the same to 0.2 kHz in 4.9's and 4.10's rounds**; the 24 at gain 70 and 80 back.  No round has tried the interference hold on a CW this close; next.  `pulses` (8 MHz noise bursts): 54 of 54 locked after; `decide`: the lock after 215 of 216 steps (the 5.5 GHz noise at 89.75 left it beating).  `iholdcheck`: no hold anywhere (A3 with it on 19 996 / 19 500 / 19 800 right against 19 999 / 19 512 / 19 792 off, no symbol error; 25 Mbaud BPSK 10 of 10 clean at each; 20 s quiet).  **`segcheck`: 180 of 180 segments clean** (at each carrier 40 at 25 Mbaud, 10 at 50, 10 at 10; at 5.0 GHz 5 of 40 and 5 of 10 read random on version 5 the round before).  `eye`: the skew by the fold 2.5 / 2.1 samples at 5.2 GHz (50 / 25 Mbaud), 3.5 / 3.0 at 5.0, 2.6 / 2.4 at 5.5 (new; like 5.2); the eye 1.000 at the recovery's point at every carrier, 0.74-0.77 at the false one; 0 of 60 recovered segments random (5.0 GHz's 25 Mbaud fold read latency 25 against 22: any lag between the rising and falling clusters fits equally well, and the skew is the same).  B210 -> board (`b210cdr`, 1 and 2 Mbaud, three runs each a carrier): 27.2 M bits, 17 of 18 runs clean, 29 mismatches in one 2 Mbaud run at 5.0 GHz (about ten bit errors: the check counts each three times); the clocks +4.7..+5.6 ppm, the carriers +0.88 / +0.90 / +0.94 ppm.  **New: from the B210, RX_CDR_SKEW reads 13-27 samples (16-34 ns) at 1 and 2 Mbaud, against 2-3.5 samples (2.5-4.4 ns) in the board's own link**, the same CMP_I at the same 0.16-0.19 V leg.  The B210 sends rectangular symbols at 4 MS/s, which its own interpolation filters round into edges far slower than the board's 10-13 ns.  A threshold offset moves the rising crossing one way and the falling one the other by offset / slew, so the skew grows with the edge's duration; a mismatch of the comparator's propagation delays would read the same few ns from any source.  So this supports REVB 30's reading (an offset, the same one as REVB 18's push).  The recovery samples half the skew later, and at 1-2 Mbaud the eye is 400-800 samples wide, so nothing is lost.  The quiet soak (04:28-05:22, 20 minutes at each carrier): 2 015 looks, no loss, slip or blip |
| 4.12 the I comparator's trim against CMP_I's skew and the unlocked loop's push, and the push measured again with the carrier on (Sat 05:38-06:14, the rounds stopped for it; `weekend.py once` with `skewtrim`, `pushscan`, `pushq`, `pullin`: `weekend/skewtrim1.log`, `pushscan1.log` (muted, stopped), `pushscan2.log`, `pullin1.log`; the rounds restarted 06:15, `weekend/rounds8.log`, with `captureauto` first) | REVB 30's proposed trim servo; whether one trim nulls the skew and the push | **CMP_I's skew follows I_OFF_F (the fast I arm's trim, -0.020 V/V on I) and RX_CDR_SKEW reads it**: at 5.0, 5.2 and 5.5 GHz, I_OFF_F stepped 1.0-3.3 V with the loop locked at each, the skew falls 1.6 / 1.2 / 1.1 samples a volt at 50 Mbaud (1.3-2.0 ns a volt: at 20 mV a volt, a crossing slope of 20-30 mV a ns, the 10-13 ns edge of REVB 20), from 3.4 / 2.4 / 2.5 samples at V_MID to 0.7 / 0.5 / 0.8 at 3.3 V, the DAC's top; the eye fold's skew agrees with RX_CDR_SKEW within 0.1-0.2 sample at every step.  **At 3.3 V the eye opens from 10-12 to 14-15 of 16 samples at 50 Mbaud and from 27-28 to 31 of 32 at 25 Mbaud**, no bit wrong (162 timing-recovered segments of 50 ms; one with 3 mismatches, at 1.0 V).  The zero would be at 3.8-4.1 V, past the DAC.  The no-carrier null (CMP_I's 50 %, 2.73 / 2.76 / 2.76 V, where A3's idle wants I_OFF_F, 3.4c) removes only about half: the rest is not the DC offset the null finds (a difference of the comparator's rising and falling delays, or something only the fast signal shows).  **The push did not follow, and the reason was the measurement.**  The release rates fell at -3.7 V/s (5.0 GHz) whatever I_OFF_F below the null, jumped to +17..+45 V/s near it and settled at -5.5 V/s above it, the same to 0.1 V/s at every offset from -40 to +40 kHz.  A sequencer step drives every bit it does not name to 1 (`costaslink_core.v`: `tx_dis = seq_out[2]` while it runs; `seq_program`'s 'missing bits are 1'), and the release step named only `loop_hold`: **the TX was muted for every timed release, here and in 3.6d's `drift_rates`**.  Those rates are the integrator against ERR with no carrier: ERR is the leg of Q that CMP_I's static state picks (1.6302 V with CMP_I high, 1.6284 V low at 5.0 GHz), and near the null CMP_I chatters on noise and lifts ERR by up to 53 mV (+123 V/s at 5.2 GHz): 3.6d's 'reversal at the null'.  `locktests._drift_rates` and the new pieces now keep the TX on (`Weekend.release_rate`); the other sequencer users (`locklab` drop, drop2, the pulsed CW) name tx_dis already, and `stages.loop_center` mutes on purpose.  **With the carrier on, the push does not depend on I_OFF_F**: 2.0-3.3 V in 0.05 V steps, the rate 30 kHz above the lock -1.2 / -3.3 / -2.4 V/s and 30 kHz below -4.1 / -6.0 / -5.6 V/s (5.0 / 5.2 / 5.5 GHz), within 0.6 V/s a volt; ERR's mean held across the beat 1.626-1.629 V at every trim.  Q_OFF_F (DAC1, 0.6-2.7 V) moves it 1.0-2.0 V/s a volt: across a beat ERR takes both Q legs in turn, and the trim moves them oppositely, so only CMP_I's 53-55 % duty is left to act on.  The muted releases against the muted ERR (Q_OFF_F walks it 41 mV) fit RC 1.020 ms (10 k x 100 nF) and **the integrator's reference V_MID at 1.6339-1.6340 V on the ADC's scale at all three carriers** (the Fluke read 1.6328 V at C131, REVB 17), 0.05-0.07 V/s residual; ERR's beat average sits 4-10 mV under it.  **What moves an unlocked loop, measured (`pullin`: held at -400..+400 kHz with the carrier on, released 3 ms, three releases a point; the same shape at all three carriers to 1-2 V/s)**: far above (+250..+400 kHz) VC falls 6.9-7.8 V/s, far below 1.6-3.1 V/s: a common -4.3..-5.5 V/s (the mid-point above) and 2.4-2.8 V/s toward the carrier on both sides, already in ERR's held mean (the detector's own response to the beat, which does not fall off from 250 to 400 kHz as the textbook pull would).  Near the lock the pull is strong, 12-13 V/s at +15 kHz and 7-8 V/s at -10 kHz.  **The rate crosses zero at the lock, at a watershed 13-16 kHz below it, at a resting place 43-49 kHz below (the false lock 3.9h found at -46..-61 kHz), and at a second watershed 94-129 kHz below; beyond that VC falls to the bottom rail.  Above the lock it never crosses: from anywhere above, VC comes down to the lock**, slowest at +30..+40 kHz (1.3-4 V/s), where drags have parked (4.5).  So the capture window's lower edge (-10 kHz in 3.9h) is the watershed, a loop let go 16-129 kHz below settles in the false lock, and one further below is lost; a search must start above the carrier and come down (3.9j's 52 of 52).  `captureauto` at 5.2 GHz (the interference hold on, a CW 2-50 kHz off at gain 80 and 89.75): 23 of 24 back; the one lost was the full-power CW 20 kHz below, which the loop **locked onto** (VC 23 kHz down during it; 6 holds, 0.18 s in all: a lock on the interferer keeps the Q windows quiet), and left between the watershed and the false lock, where the map says it would rest: an interferer inside the capture window is not interference to the hold, so a lock detector and a search (REVB 21, 25) are needed even with it |
| 4.13 the interference hold against a CW near the carrier (`captureauto`, round 1 of `weekend/rounds8.log`, Sat 06:16-07:30 at 5.2, 5.0 and 5.5 GHz; the CW 2-50 kHz either side of the carrier at gain 80 and 89.75, three seconds, the B210's own LO leak parked 2 MHz away; the hold at its defaults, 20 ms release) | does the hold save what `capture` loses at 5.5 GHz (4.11)? | **No, and near the carrier it does harm.**  5.2 GHz: 23 of 24 back (the free loop's rounds 35 of 36); the loss is 4.12's, a full-power CW 20 kHz below that the loop locked onto.  **5.0 GHz: 14 of 24 back, 10 of the 12 at full power lost, VC dragged 14-63 kHz down; in the same round without the hold (`capture`) 36 of 36, the 12 at full power with VC unmoved (to 0.1 kHz), as in all six earlier rounds at the same DSA 19.75-21.75 (72 of 72)**.  5.5 GHz: 7 of 24 back: at full power 12 of 12 lost, dragged 23-92 kHz down, as without the hold (4.11); at gain 80, 5 of 12 lost where the free loop came back 24 of 24 in every earlier round.  The holds were many and short: 1-12 (once 53) a CW, 2.8 s held of the 3 s.  Why: the busy rule (two Q window comparators each changing 4 or more times in a 2.56 us window) is built for beats of about 1 MHz and more; a CW 2-50 kHz off beats with a period of 20 us to 0.5 ms, and reads busy only in the comparators' chatter at its slow crossings, now and then.  At 5.0 GHz the locked loop rides such a CW out by itself (the CW captures CMP_I, but sign(I) x Q averages to nothing, so VC stays: the free loop's 'beating' is CMP_I toggling, not a loss).  Held, the loop keeps VC but its phase walks; released 20 ms after a chatter burst into a CW stronger than the carrier, it cannot pull back in, drifts unlocked (4.12's -4.5 V/s and more), is held again further off, and is left 14-63 kHz below: inside 4.12's false-lock basin (-16 to -129 kHz), where it rests.  So a hold that fires intermittently is worse than none.  Tried next, both from firmware (the rounds restarted 07:39, `weekend/rounds9.log`, these first at each carrier): `captureauto2`, the release at 200 ms, to bridge the chatter bursts into one hold; `captureauto3` and `pullauto3`, the busy rule blind to slow beats (a 1024-clock window, thresh 32: 16 changes of each comparator in 10.24 us, which a 1 MHz beat gives and a slow beat's chatter should not), checked against the dragging CWs of 1-40 MHz that the hold exists for |
| 4.13a the hold's two variants near the carrier (`weekend/rounds9.log`, Sat 07:31-09:09, round 1 with `captureauto2`, `captureauto3` and `pullauto3` first at each carrier) | which setting does no harm near the carrier and still catches the dragging CWs? | **The 200 ms release is the best of them, and better than no hold.**  CWs 2-50 kHz off at gain 80 and 89.75, back of 24 at 5.2 / 5.0 / 5.5 GHz: **no hold 23 / 24 / 12 (59 of 72; `capture` in the same rounds, 5.5 GHz from 4.11); the hold at its 20 ms release 23 / 14 / 7 (44 of 72, 4.13); at a 200 ms release 24 / 24 / 13 (61 of 72); blind to slow beats (a 1024-clock window, thresh 32) 23 / 23 / 12 (58 of 72)**.  With 200 ms each CW was one hold to its end (3.0-3.2 s), VC within 2 kHz but once 14, and the loop back 0.22-0.31 s after (0.10 s at 20 ms: the release itself).  The slow-blind rule mostly did not fire at full power (0 holds), so the loop rode the CW out as the free loop does; it still fired on some CWs at gain 80 and twice in bursts of 42 and 91.  Neither saves 5.5 GHz at full power: the CW drags the loop 51-92 kHz down within the first milliseconds, before a hold can start (the 200 ms hold then keeps the dragged VC, up to max_s, 5 s), and that needs the lock detector and a search.  **`pullauto3`, the slow-blind rule against pull's dragging CWs of 1-40 MHz: 120 of 120 back at the three carriers** (one 3.02 s hold each), as the default's 280 of 280.  Before the 200 ms release becomes the driver's default, it must not hurt under bursts, where releases closer than 200 ms merge into one long hold: `pulsesauto` (20 ms) and `pulsesauto2` (200 ms), 8 MHz noise bursts of 10 us / 1 ms, 100 us / 10 ms, 1 ms / 20 ms and 1 ms / 102.4 ms (a WiFi beacon's rhythm) with A3's decisions counted, first in `weekend/rounds10.log` (09:16); `captureauto` and `captureauto3` now name 20 ms, so they stay comparable whatever the default |
| 4.14 the interference hold under bursts, and its persistence (overlay ID 0x434C0007 from `3c76423`, Vivado 2022.1, WNS +0.011 ns, WHS +0.032 ns, deployed 10:05 to `~/costaslink_persist/`, both drivers `c17e52c54a77`; `weekend/rounds10.log` 09:11-10:02 and `rounds11.log` 10:05-10:15 at 5.2 GHz; 8 MHz noise bursts at gain 80 and 89.75 for 3 s with A3's 20 000 events counted, two runs a setting, against the 27 free runs of the weekend) | does a 200 ms release, or a hold that waits for a streak, cost anything under WiFi-like bursts? | **Under bursts every hold costs the decisions, and the free loop needs none.**  Free, at every pattern (10 us every 1 ms, 100 us every 10 ms, 1 ms every 20 ms): A3 21 000-32 000 right, at most 224 missed, the loop locked after in 27 of 27.  The hold at its 20 ms release: held 2.5-2.75 s of the 3 s (each burst re-arms it inside the release), A3 1 000-17 000 right and 5 000-18 000 missed; at 200 ms the same, worse; at a beacon's rhythm (1 ms every 102.4 ms) 20 ms holds 0.53 s and A3 keeps 17 000-20 000, 200 ms holds the whole 2.75 s and A3 gets 900-1 100.  The hold does not gate the decisions (ihold.v drives LOOP_HOLD only): held, the RX oscillator walks off the carrier's phase (0.1 rad in 6 ms, PATHS.md), so A3 misses from about 50 ms into a hold.  **So 200 ms cannot be the default** (4.13a's best near the carrier), and the hold as built is a net loss under bursty interference.  **The persistence (`ihold.v` `persist`, RX_IHOLD_PERSIST: a hold only after that many busy windows in a row; `tb_ihold.v`: a near CW's chatter 12 holds at 0, none at 195; 10 and 100 us bursts none; a broken streak none; a lasting beat held after 194 windows) at 0.5 ms**: bursts of 10 and 100 us no hold at all and A3 as free (20 700-23 400 right, at most 165 missed); 1 ms bursts still hold at full power; near the carrier (`captureauto4`) 23 of 24, as free.  **But the dragging CWs (`pullauto4`): 29 of 40 back, the 11 lost all below the carrier (-5 to -40 MHz, gain 80-89.75), VC dragged 21-169 kHz in the first half millisecond**: up to about 340 kHz a ms, 20 times the 13-19 kHz a ms I had estimated from the delay's push alone (the error swung to its rail).  Skipping 100 us bursts needs a streak over 100 us; stopping a drag needs a hold within about 30 us.  The better lever is the release: hold for a few times as long as the windows were busy (a 100 us burst a half-millisecond hold, the phase barely moving; a near CW's chatter microsecond holds, the loop as good as free; a 3 s CW to its end plus 20 ms), for the gateware with the lock supervisor (ID 8).  Meanwhile a 20 us streak (8 windows) is on the bench (`pullauto5`, `pulsesauto5`, `captureauto5`, `weekend/persist20.log`) |
| 4.15 the link's automatic recovery, proven from the PC, and the board's own status page (Jason 09:50: the boards run unattended, operated by physicists who do not know PLLs, 'this part just needs to work reliably without intervention'; `bringup/autolock.py abuse`, `autolock/abuse1.log` 10:37-10:57, `abuse2.log` 10:59-11:24, `abuse3.log` 11:26-11:36; overlay ID 7) | every way the bench can break the lock, each followed by the recovery until it is locked again | **Every forced loss came back by itself, in 1.7-3.0 s and on the first start: 0 of 171 disturbances not recovered (and 63 burst trains, 234 trials in all).**  The recovery: when the lock reads lost, hold the loop, centre its integrator (5.8 GHz, the TX muted, `loop_center`'s trims), set VC 20 kHz above the last VC it was locked at and release it: from above, the unlocked loop always comes down into the lock (4.12's map).  At each of 5.2, 5.0 and 5.5 GHz, three times each: released 45 kHz below (the false lock, beating) back in 1.7-1.8 s; 300 kHz below (VC on its bottom rail) 2.2-3.0 s; 300 kHz above: locked again by itself in 0.1 s, nothing to do; the carrier gone 1, 10 and 60 s (TX_DIS, the loop free) 2.3-3.0 s at 5.0 and 5.2 GHz (at 5.5 GHz the muted TX's leak, about -40 dBm, still held the lock at DSA 9.75: not a real test there); the B210's full-power CW 10 MHz below for 3 s (pull's drag) 2.3-3.0 s; its CW 20 kHz below at full power once 2.3 s, otherwise locked again unaided.  **The detector had to learn one thing: noise bursts.**  With the loss judged on the comparators alone (CMP_I's chatter), each 10 s train of bursts drew one needless recovery (about 3 s of link lost for nothing; 9 of 9 trains in `abuse1`); with 'or lost 5 s however near', the dense trains still did (10 us every 1 ms, 100 us every 10 ms: 18 of 18, the 1 ms and beacon-rhythm trains 0 of 18).  **Judged on VC alone, more than 8 kHz from the learned lock for half a second, no train drew one (0 of 18, `abuse3`)** and every real loss still did: each moves VC tens of kHz, and a lost loop cannot stay within 8 kHz of the lock, where it pulls in (4.12).  'Locked', which the learned VC follows, also counts both Q windows quiet with Q near its middle, so it holds under the link's own BPSK data.  **On the board (`gateware/driver/linkd.py`, in the board server since 11:40, `netbus.py serve --linkd`)**: the same algorithm on the driver alone, no PC, no network; active whenever no client has made a request for 60 s (whoever drives the board owns it), a loop held by software left alone (a test, or the bench's quiet state), a failed recovery backing off 5 s doubling to 60 s (the far board off).  Its end-to-end test (`autolock.py linkd`: the board left alone, then disturbed, watched only through the status page) is running.  **The status page (`gateware/driver/dashboard.py`, http://192.168.8.142:8080/, since 11:07)**: read-only, looking between the server's requests under its lock (the free-running edge counters, the holds, the slow ADC and detectors; a running request is never waited on); a status card in words (the state, its cause, what to do, who is driving, what the recovery is doing and its counts); plots each with a help button, an arrow on the axis pointing the good way and green / yellow / red bands (Jason 10:15); a coarse constellation from the slow ADC with the locked idle's places drawn; two hours kept, a daily log on the PYNQ.  Its first real readings set the 'unwanted power' bands: the Q arm's average 20.8-23.9 dB over the noise locked, about 54 dB lost (the carrier beats through Q) |
| 4.16 the recovery on the board itself, end to end (`gateware/driver/linkd.py` in the board server, `netbus.py serve --linkd` since 11:40; `autolock.py linkd`, `autolock/linkd2.log`, 11:42-12:30 at 5.2, 5.0 and 5.5 GHz) | does linkd, with no PC behind it, bring the link back? | **Yes: 23 losses, 23 recovered by linkd, none failed.**  The link locked, then left alone until linkd was active, then disturbed and watched only through the status page (not a client of the board).  By linkd's own record: the false lock 45 kHz below back in 1.3 s, the rail 1.8-2.4 s, the carrier gone 10 s 2.4 s, the B210's full-power CW 10 MHz below 1.9-5.4 s (twice two starts: linkd saw the loss during the CW and its first start, with the CW still on, did not lock; the second, after it, did). The other disturbances need one request to make them, and linkd then waits out its minute of 'a program is driving': back 61-65 s after them, as designed (and the second drag of each carrier, 61-62 s: the test's own check of the lock just before it paused linkd, and the page lagged a second behind; the test now waits for a minute of silence).  The 7 'not recovered' were no losses at all: the CW 20 kHz below rode out at every carrier (6), and at 5.5 GHz the muted TX's leak kept the lock (1); linkd read locked throughout (the test now counts that a pass).  **The first try found a design fault: the bench watchdog reads the detectors every half minute, and the server counted every request as 'driving', so linkd never got its quiet minute; requests that only look (pings, register reads, the detectors, the holds' and timing recovery's state, the ADC) no longer count.**  Next: `unattended.py` (the B210's random interference for hours, nothing touching the board) |

(4) The PYNQ answered at 10:24:30 and ID 12 loaded at 54 s (the manager 'operating'), but the page said 'firmware' and the ID register read a different small signed number each time (0x00FFFF89, 0x00000034, ...), as did CTRL, SPI_DATA and SPI_CTRL, also through `/dev/mem` on the PYNQ with the server bypassed. A race at every cold boot, lost this time; `costaslink-netbus` now starts after `bootpy.service`.  The morning's DMA failure is still unexplained (the same file loaded at once after the power cycle).  (5) ID 12 on the board: A3 199 999 of 199 999 right, QPSK 7.5e6 symbols all right, BPSK 50 Mbaud 3.8e8 bits, **OOK** 2 Mbaud 8.0e6 bits and 10 Mbaud 4.0e7 bits, no error. | 4.34 where the receiver's corner error lives (Jason 22:10: 'learn as much as you can about this issue'; REVB 38; the B210 as the exact source over the air as in 4.31, the board's TX muted and its loop locked to the B210; `bringup/revb38/revb38.py`, `revb38b.py`, `revb38c.py` (their logs and JSON in `data/board_A/b210link/revb38*`), 2026-11-02 22:21-22:44 PST) | the corners' asymmetry (Q 7-15 % smaller on one side of I, the corners not symmetric about their centre): the RF chain, the demodulator, the arms, the loop, or the measurement? | **Not the RF chain, not static: it is a bilinear I x Q term that grows with how long a corner is held, from about 2 % at 0.5 us to 9-13 % at 2-3.5 us, the same on both lock points in the board's own frame; at the link's 200 ns symbols it extrapolates to about 1 %.**  In the board's physical frame (the four corners about their own centre): (A) the corners sent at 1.41, 0.71, 0.35 and 0.18 of the idle's amplitude, 2 us wide in 4 (lock point -, DSA 13.75, leg 192 mV): |Q| at I < 0 over I > 0 = 1.099, 1.078, 1.025, 1.026; |I| at Q > 0 over Q < 0 = 0.86, 0.91, 0.93, 0.97; (C) the same on the comparators' own plateaus (rxcal's duty steps, no scope and no transitions): the two +Q corners at +202.0 and +219.5 mV (8 %), the two -Q at 180.5 and 192.8; at 0.71 the +Q pair 96.2 and 107.0 (11 %): the receiver, not the clustering; (B) the corners 0.5, 2 and 3.5 us wide in a 4 us period: 1.016 / 0.957, 1.088 / 0.863, 1.131 / 0.841 (20 us in 40 rotated the corners past the clustering's seeds: unusable); (D) the LNA 6 dB hotter and 6 dB cooler at the same demodulator level (the B210 +-6 dB, the DSA +-6 dB): 1.106 / 0.886 and 1.100 / 0.855, unchanged: not the LNA, the DSA or the gain blocks; (E, lock point +, `revb38c.py`): free, 2 us: 1.169 and 1.149 / 1.066 and 1.094 (4.31's 1.125 / 1.09 again), 3.5 us: 1.136 and 1.150 / 1.111 and 1.110; **the loop held** during the pattern (the decisive test: a frozen phase cannot be moved by a corner's I x Q): unusable on rev A, since each hold and release moves the VCXO by the hold's charge injection (4.22's 1.3 mV, about 600 Hz), 10 degrees inside one 50 us capture (the clusters' spread 13-24 mV against 10 free).  What the structure says: the Q term's sign is fixed in the board's frame (Q larger where the board's I is negative) and the I term's flips with the lock point; both are products of I and Q, which is exactly the Costas loop's error signal, and both grow over microseconds: the loop's own motion during a long corner (the VCXO's phase), or that error signal coupling into the arms with a microsecond time constant (through the loop's shared mid-point V_MID, REVB 17, or the sign mux's loading of the Q arm's legs).  A pure rotation of the constellation gives no asymmetry to first order, so the coupling is the better candidate, but the two are not separated.  What settles it: a probe tomorrow on ERR MON and V_MID during the corners and on the ADL5380's outputs ahead of the arms (REVB_DESIGN_SPACE 6, item 4), or a hold with no charge injection (REVB U4's integrator reset).  What it means now: 4.31's '10 %' was measured with 2 us corners and is mostly this; the link's symbols are 200 ns and the calibration's states 200 ns, where the term is about 1 %; 1 of 8's margins (REVB 42) must be measured with 200 ns symbols, not microsecond states.  Also fixed: rxscope's cluster seeds assumed a 50 % duty (wrong at any other width: `6363e45`) |

Not plain compression: a stage that goes unstable or collapses when driven at 3-4.4 GHz (out of the band; the LNA's match and the gain blocks' are not designed there).  To see it: a spectrum analyzer on RX MON while the VNA or a generator drives RX ANT at 4 GHz, -45 to -35 dBm, looking for an oscillation's line.
