| 4.47 the night over the dishes, hour 1 (2026-11-03 23:10 to 11-04 00:25 PST by the laptop's clock; 5200 MHz, the self-test's cycle; `bringup/night.py` (new): survey, levels, the AGC, carriers, gaps; every page event logged to `bringup/data/board_A/night/`) | the band, the link against its level, the QPSK misses, the comparators' levels (Jason 23:40: 'be sure that you are setting the Comparator and other levels correctly') | **The link is clean only within about 6 dB of its setup level, and linkd could leave it crippled after a slow fade (fixed); the band seen by the grids is quiet, the loudest signals in it the board's own TX synthesizer.**  **The survey** (the RX synthesizer stepped 4.9-6.0 GHz by 5 MHz, the TX disabled, the loop held, V_T2 scanned on the slow Q arm about its measured middle: the driver's survey() scans only above V_MID and tonight's middle is 23 mV below, so it resolved nothing): with the TX synthesizer on 5200 MHz, tones of -91 dBm (5200, in 7.5 MHz), -85 (5225), **-68 (5465)** and -75 to -82 dBm (5875-5880) **although TX_DIS was set**; with it parked at 3000 MHz all gone, and the band at most +5 dB over the receiver's noise everywhere (4.9-4.97 GHz +4-5; WiFi bursts about 1 % at 5525).  The laptop sees 88-93 networks, 34-39 at 5 GHz (channels 149, 36, 64, 108 strongest): off the grids' axis.  **The level** (`night.py levels`: the DSA stepped as nodrive, linkd and the self-test on duty, 3 min each), BPSK BER at 2-50 Mbaud / QPSK right / single symbols: DSA 8: all <= 2e-7 (50 Mbaud 7e-8) / 80 % / A1, A1w, A2 100 %, A3 75 %; DSA 10 (the best): <= 3.5e-7 / **84 %**; DSA 12.5 (setup's): <= 9e-7 / 48-77 %; DSA 15: 1e-6 to 1.6e-5 / 56 %; **DSA 18 (leg 44-88 mV): 25 and 50 Mbaud 0.18-0.20, A1 exactly 50 % right (CMP_I stuck), A1w 0**; DSA 21: only 2 Mbaud (2.7e-6); 24: lock losses; 27-31.75: no carrier.  OOK 1e-5 to 9e-5 at 10 Mbaud, 25 Mbaud 2e-5 at best.  So about 6 dB below the setup level the fast decisions fail while the loop still reads locked: a fixed offset in the fast path against a shrinking leg, not noise.  PATHS.md's 'the loop's level regulator holds the demodulator at -20 dBm' is not on rev A: the DSA is set once, at setup.  **QPSK's misses** (the self-test's 'levels' step, A3, lock point -1, the windows the plain rule's): the +Q states sit at 1.692 V against QP's threshold 1.695; one +I corner at 1.674 against IP's 1.690; the two -I corners 29 mV apart (1.558, 1.587): the corners' Q about 0.27 of the leg, not 0.7 (4.34's distortion); one corner decided as another 75 % of the time on lock point +1 too.  **Slips** to the other lock point without a loss: 3 in 27 minutes (23:03, 23:25, 23:30).  **A linkd fault, fixed:** after the gradual fade to DSA 24-27 and back, linkd's leg stayed 13.7 mV for good (the carrier 156 mV, the windows set for 15 mV, the page 'locked', linkd 'watching'): observe()'s lock test scales Q's tolerance (0.35 of the leg) by the learned leg, Q's ordinary offset exceeds 4.8 mV, so 'locked' never came and the leg was never learned again.  `linkd.py` now scales by the larger of the learned leg and the I arm's deflection (a loss or a false lock still fails: I falls to its middle); deployed on the PYNQ 00:10 (the old one `linkd.py.bak_20261104`), the server restarted (setup 47 s, DSA 12.5, leg 134 mV): the same gradual fade (15, 18, 21, 24 dB, 80 s each: the leg learned down to 41 mV, 4 losses at 21-24) and back: the leg learned again to 130 mV within 60 s |

| 4.50 carrier gaps over the dishes, the holdover off and on (2026-11-04 03:40-05:07 PST by the laptop's clock; 5200 MHz, DSA 8.5 (the AGC's), the self-test's cycle; `night.py gaps --n 20` and `--n 20 --holdover`: TX_DIS for 10 ms-60 s log-uniform as nodrive, 30-60 s apart) | does over-the-air behave as the cabled gaps of 4.20-4.22 did (REVB 31)? | **Yes, with one loss under the holdover.**  Off: **20 of 20 gaps lost the lock, the shortest 11 ms**, the oscillator 44-750 kHz below the carrier by the time it came back (the integrator running on ERR's no-carrier offset, REVB 31); locked again by itself 1.7-3.6 s after the carrier returned for gaps under 1 s and 2-7 s after it for longer ones (linkd 'waiting for the carrier', then its recovery); one 30.7 s gap needed a second try (12 s).  On: **19 of 20 rode through with no loss** (10 ms to 22 s), one of them (the 22 s) back half a cycle over (cabled: 14 of 33 slipped); **one lost: 1.7 s into a 15.8 s mute, the oscillator 398 kHz below, back 7 s after the carrier with one failed try**: the hold let go (or never took) with the carrier still gone.  The page's states could not say which, so `night.py gaps` now records the holdover's holds and held seconds across each gap.  Candidates: a burst on the comparators releasing the hold for its 1 ms, after which the free loop passes the watershed in 40-50 ms (REVB 31); or the TX synthesizer's leak (REVB 49), which stays on through TX_DIS and beats against a drifting loop.  **But the holdover slips the loop under OOK** (found 06:05 in the night's event log; the gap rows above only looked 2 s past each gap): in those 16 minutes 17 half-cycle slips, 12 of them in the idle look 1-2 s after an OOK round (12 of 36 OOK rounds, 2 of 15 calibrations, 0 of 72 BPSK and symbol rounds), against 0 slips after 123 OOK rounds and 50 calibrations with it off in the 53 minutes before: OOK's runs of off symbols (and the calibration's window sweeps) meet the hold's 'no window high for most of 256 clocks', so the loop is held and released mid-traffic and comes back on either point.  For deployment (IMPROVEMENTS 8): the holdover turns seconds of outage into none for most gaps, but as set it must not run with OOK; the one miss also argues for linkd (or lockmon) to hold the loop when no carrier is seen, not only the windows' rule |

Setup now tries 20 kHz above when 60 does not lock (linkd `SETUP_RELEASE_HZ`, deployed 05:41, sha 602d93f1c1b91596, the previous copy `linkd.py.bak_20261104b`): 4500 and 4525 MHz set up and run clean.**  The map with the old setup: 4450 locked (DSA 13.75, clean to 25 Mbaud), 4475 locked only at DSA 23.75 (every lower step failed) with BPSK 0.15 at 10 Mbaud, 4500 and 4525 never, 4550 locked (15.75, clean).  Not the synthesizers (both lock detects high at every carrier), not the link's strength (the beat on the arm 191 mV at DSA 14 at 4500, against 106 at 5200).  The release replayed with the integrator mid-range throughout (u 1.8-2.9 V): at 4500, from +60 and +40 kHz the loop settles 44.7 kHz above (CMP_I 10 000 edges in 10 ms, VC still), from +20 and 0 it locks, from -20 to -60 it settles 39.5 kHz below (REVB 18's false lock); at 4525 likewise (+34.2 / -38.7 kHz); at 4450 from +60 to 0 it locks and only below is false (-50 kHz). How 18 of the 19 held gaps up to 22 s kept their phase over the dishes (4.50) with such a drift is open |

| 4.54 the T/R switch on one board: what mutes the transmitter for its own receiver, and how fast (2026-11-04 08:57-09:37 PST by the laptop's clock; Jason cabled TX ANT -> SW TX (J402), SW RX (J403) -> RX ANT, ANT (J401) -> 50 ohm, short cables, the dishes off; 5200 MHz; `switch_recovery.py --cabled`, then `switch_probe.py --cabled` (dsop, switch, null, depth, quiet, det, lo) at DSA 31.75, 24, 20, 15 and 10; the loop held, linkd's windows; times from the capture, 1.25 ns a sample) | can the board's receiver hear a reply ~280 ns after its own transmission through the HMC536 (the two-board time-division test over the dishes), and what does the switch, DSOP or the baseband do towards it? | **The switch does not protect the receiver: only muting the TX does.**  With the TX on, the carrier reaches RX ANT about equally in either switch state (locked and held: 0.15 / 0.15 V at DSA 31.75, 0.31 / 0.35 V at DSA 24; the log detector -21..-22.6 dBm in both, about 20 dB over the dish link's -41 dBm), as the VNA said (REVB 50: SW TX to SW RX 25 dB down in either state); flipping TR_A changes the windows' pattern 61-67 ns after its register (a transient at DSA 31.75 and 15; at DSA 10 the vector also ends a little different in the two states).  So `switch_recovery.py`'s first answer (the windows quiet 46-58 ns after TR_A, 76-88 ns after TX_DIS, at DSA 31.75-10) was **DSOP's turn-off, not the switch's**: with the switch held in either state, TX_DIS high quiets the windows 72-77 ns after its register at DSA 31.75, 80-85 at DSA 20-10 (the leak there 10-22 dB over the windows), and TX_DIS low lights them 112-126 ns after (the ADL5375 datasheet: 100 ns off to 10 %, 220 ns on to 90 %).  The ladder pins' readback lags their register by 24 samples (30 ns: output and input registers and pads), so from the pad DSOP is about 45-55 ns off and 80-95 ns on.  **The baseband null (the ladders at (0, 0), DSOP on) is three times faster**: the windows light 15-29 ns after the pins and go quiet 18-30 ns after (34-40 ns at DSA 10, the arms overdriven), the receive chain's own latency (the eye's 21-22 samples, 4.10) being most of it.  **Depth:** the null's coherent residual (the I/Q ADC, locked then held, against the zero measured with both mutes) is 0.13-0.39 mV against a 0.15-0.35 V carrier, at least 52-68 dB down (the ADC's 1-3 LSB), and DSOP off the same (**4.56: not a reading of the null: the held loop's frame moves across it**); but the log detector's band power with the null is only **33-41 dB under the carrier** (-55..-62 dBm), 9-20 dB over the floor that DSOP off (and both) reads.  (The detector is the Q arm AC-coupled at 16 kHz, PATHS.md: noise reaches it, a locked and held carrier does not; the carrier's -21..-22.6 dBm was read while the held loop beat at ~34 kHz in the false lock below, and the null and the floor read 6-7 dB lower in a later, locked run.)  So the null leaves broadband power the TX chain makes while DSOP is on (**4.55, on the analyzer: not noise, but REVB 5's 25 MHz sidebands and the carrier of a null trimmed on RX MON, 33 dB down at TX ANT; the TX's noise at the null is about -144 dBm/Hz there, inside the spec**), the same for all four (0, 0) codes (01 / 10 on each axis, to 0.2 dB), some 30 dB over the ADL5375's specified floor (-153..-160 dBm/Hz, +14.3 dB of U202, the 31 dB path: about -88 dBm in the +-94 MHz band, est).  **Part of it needs the LO**: with the TX synthesizer's RFOUTA off (register 4 resent with RFA_EN clear, DSOP on) the band power falls 4-11 dB, and **part does not** (9-13 dB over the floor until DSOP is off): baseband noise mixed up by the LO (the ladder pins' drive, most likely) plus the output stage's own; not seen on an analyzer yet (4.55: the part the LO mixes up is the sidebands; with RFOUTA off the TX VCO still puts -29 dBm at fc on TX ANT).  **The LO's part fires the windows at the operating DSA**: at DSA 10 with the null alone, CMP_IP 518 times in 328 us of forced captures (pulses ~2 ns) at 09:16 and 548 in 492 us at 09:36, in the switch's TX state only; at 09:33, after linkd had recovered a lost lock and recalibrated, all four windows 150-290 times each in 492 us in both states; none with RFOUTA off, none with DSOP off, none at DSA 15 and 31.75.  The null's 'quiet after' at DSA 10 had stragglers 0.27-18 us late, these.  **For the two-board test over the dishes (round trip ~280 ns plus the prover's turnaround):** the verifier clears its own TX in time with DSOP, noise included, by ~85 ns after its register (~200 ns to spare); the null first is faster (~50-70 ns) but leaves the noise above until DSOP follows.  **The prover on one antenna has no usable mute on rev A**: DSOP turns on in 112-126 ns from the register, against a 20-60 ns turnaround; the null leaves its own TX's noise 15-20 dB under a -41 dBm challenge at its RX ANT, and that noise fires its windows at the operating DSA (above); and its switch has to reach TX before the reply goes out at full power (the HMC536's 30 ns t_on adds to the turnaround).  Rev B: REVB 55.  Then the dishes go back (cable A's SMA end on TX ANT, B's on RX ANT) and the server is restarted |
| 4.55 the TX on the DSA875: what the null leaves (2026-11-04 11:10-11:55 PST by the laptop's clock; the DSA875 on the laptop's USB; first on ANT (J401) with TX ANT -> SW TX, SW RX -> RX ANT as in 4.54 (TR_A 0 puts SW TX on the analyzer, 2.3 dB under TX ANT), then (Jason, 11:40) on TX ANT itself, RX ANT on 50 ohm, SW TX, SW RX and ANT open; 5200 MHz; `tx_spectrum.py --cabled --at ant`, then `--at txant --null-vr 0.2482 0.2712` and with `--att 0`; the loop held, the point held (TX_MODE 5); one-off scripts for the lines' frequencies, VR against the null and the receiver with RX ANT loaded) | where is 4.54's broadband power, the null's residue (REVB 55's first step)? | **It is not noise: it is REVB 5's 25 MHz sidebands, plus the carrier a null trimmed on the receiver leaves.**  (1) **Lines at fc +- odd multiples of 25 MHz**: at TX ANT -30.4 / -30.7 dBm at -/+25 MHz, -33.8 / -35.7 at 75, -36.6 / -38.8 at 125, on down to 225 (-48 / -51 on ANT), against the carrier's +8.1 dBm (-38 dBc at 25 MHz); the same absolute level with the carrier on, at either (0, 0) code (0101, 1010) and with the RX synthesizer's RFOUTA off; gone (under -65 dBm) with the TX synthesizer's RFOUTA off and with DSOP; their spacing fc / 208 within ~20 Hz with the carrier 1.0 ppm low (the held loop drifted 50-100 Hz during it).  The TCXO's 25 MHz on the modulator's baseband, as stage 2.3 found ('+-25 MHz sidebands', REVB 5).  Together -25.6 dBm in +-94 MHz without the carrier, 33 dB under it: 4.54's "33-41 dB under the carrier" on the log detector (25 and 75 MHz tones on the Q arm, over its 16 kHz coupling); they need the TX LO, which is why RFOUTA off quieted the windows.  (2) **The carrier at the null is -25.3 dBm at TX ANT, 33.4 dB under, with link.json's VR_I 0.2554 / VR_Q 0.278 V**; it follows VR as a baseband offset does (12 dB over +-12 mV on ANT, the slope the ladder predicts: 0.79 mV at the node per mV of VR against 330 mV for a), and a grid search on the analyzer puts the TX ANT null at VR_I -7.2 mV, VR_Q -6.8 mV (0.2482 / 0.2712 V; -7.2 / -6.0 on ANT): **-53 dBm, 61 dB under the carrier, 26 dB deeper**.  link.json's VR is `campaign.py cal`'s (3.8e), which nulled the carrier on **RX MON** through the two 20 dB pads: the receiver's input, where the board's own leak adds to the padded path, so that null cancels the leak with a deliberate offset at TX ANT.  VR put back after each run (link.json is Jason's call).  (3) **Between the lines the null is the TX's own floor**: at 0 dB input attenuation the trimmed null reads -140.7..-141.6 dBm/Hz 0.5-20 MHz out against the analyzer's -143.4..-144.7 with DSOP and both RFOUTAs off, so the TX's noise is about -144 dBm/Hz at TX ANT (the ADL5375's -153..-160 plus U202's 14.3 dB, est: inside it), -61 dBm in +-94 MHz, 35 dB under the sidebands.  No lines at the FPGA's clocks over 10 MHz-7.5 GHz (1 MHz RBW, peak; the floor -63 dBm at 10 dB attenuation), only one-off bursts in single sweeps (0.86, 4.0-4.6, 6.0-6.8 GHz).  (4) **RFOUTA off is no mute**: the TX VCO still puts -28.8 dBm at fc on TX ANT (it stays with the RX synthesizer moved to 5175 MHz), and the modulator without its LO makes a comb 25 MHz apart at 0.67-1.3 and 2.44-2.67 GHz (-37 dBm at 2457 MHz), in both cablings and only in that state; DSOP leaves -41 dBm at fc (-52 with the TX RFOUTA off too).  (5) **Not explained**: on the switch (4.54) the receiver read the null at least 52 dB under the carrier and moved at most 1.5 mV against its 0.8 V carrier when VR moved +-12 mV (the analyzer: 12 dB; 18-40 mV expected at the receiver); through the board alone (RX ANT on 50 ohm: the carrier 25 / 47 / 72 mV at DSA 31.75 / 9.75 / 0, 15-25 dB under the switch's path) it reads the null 29-31 dB under the carrier, about as TX ANT does.  A run on the switch with the lock checked at every point would settle it (one try here had the loop off its lock: the carrier 0.06 V).  **(4.56: it is the method, not the receiver: the held loop's frame does not hold across the null, VC moving 3-11 mV with the received carrier, so the I/Q ADC reads the null's residual as a kHz beat.)**  **For REVB 55**: with REVB 5 fixed and the null trimmed at the output, the null leaves the TX's noise (-61 dBm in +-94 MHz at TX ANT, about -86 dBm at RX ANT through rev A's 25 dB switch, near the receiver's -88, est) and -53 dBm of carrier (a DC offset on the arms, about -78 dBm at RX ANT): fast, and close to quiet enough.  The analyzer left on TX ANT at fc +-250 MHz, 30 dB attenuation; the board as before (CTRL 0x8, DSA 9.75, link.json's VR, both synthesizers locked) |

| 4.60 the two-board test infrastructure, and what it found first (2026-11-04 17:00-17:38 PST by the laptop's clock; the cabling as 4.59; Jason: 'Test the PRBS, idle to 1-in-N and vary every parameter ... how fast can the Prover turn around and reply to the Verifier some simple function of its one of 4 challenge ... how consistent ... how low latency is communication of bitstreams', then 'Build the infrastructure to test this with all relevant parameters varied ... the whole band ... in and out of the wifi interference ... all of your constellations ... the Comparator thresholds') | can the dish link carry a prover's round trip, and what do the knobs do? | (1) **The B -> A soak's 25 minutes** (16:59-17:16): 1684.6 Mbit, 354 errors (2.1e-7), all in 25 of 841 1 s windows, A locked at -55.3 dBm in all 14 looks.  The windows with errors come every 25-30 s with gaps (17:01:51, 02:21, 02:46, 04:22, 04:50, 05:19, 05:45, 06:13 ...) and in two bursts 2-9 s after the WiFi monitor's 10-minute 5.1-5.9 GHz sweep at 0 dB attenuation (17:01:16-23, 17:11:17-23); the watchdog's detector reads on A (every 34.5 s) and the monitor's 5293 MHz bursts (the laptop's channel) do not line up to the second: open.  (2) **No round trip over the dishes with this cabling** (REVB 55): each board's switch block passes its own TX to its own RX 25 dB down in either state, about -17 dBm against the far board's -55, and no mute is fast and quiet enough for the prover; the prover needs its own two antennas (REVB 55 (a)).  So the prover's turnaround is measured in **loopback through the switch block itself** (each board's own carrier at RX ANT through SW TX -> SW RX, no cable change; lock at DSA 29.75, leg 170 mV), the far board muted with both synthesizers parked.  (3) **`bringup/twoboard.py`** (89527df): links set up and kept with linkd's own setup and recover run from the laptop (the receiver's TX muted, the unused synthesizers 700 MHz away, REVB 57); prbs on each comparator (BPSK on CMP_I, on-off on IP / IN, the flip on IP, the Q bit and Q on-off on QP); idle-to-1-in-N events per message with the receiver's table cut to that message (a decision is then a right one) and the event-to-event histogram; the prover in loopback (counter statistics over 5000 stimuli, every challenge, and captures checking each reply on the ladder pins and as received); the band; the night's program.  Every record carries its WiFi tags.  (4) **Two things it had to learn** (IMPROVEMENTS 'Across two boards'): **the comparators' centres** (linkd's Rice fit fell back to 1.65 V on every window: no noise edges at DSA 31.75 with no carrier), so each window is now calibrated from the carrier, its level bisected at (1, 0), (-1, 0) and the Q steps (as events: a held corner turns the loop); and **the mirror** (REVB 9's inverted I arm): a receiver sees (s I, -s Q) at linkd's lock, not a plain 180 (17:30: asking for (1, -1) on side -1 raised Q 70 mV), so the server's schemes by name have their Q points the wrong way; twoboard sends mapped codes and keeps the receiver's tables nominal, and drives the prover's sync reply and fast path itself (FP_MAP written directly).  `events(count=0)` sends no events (the first calibration found no Q step).  (5) **The prover, board B in loopback, 5200 MHz, A3 (the one-of-4 corners), reply k xor 1** (17:36): the **fast path 25.0 ns** RX ANT to TX ANT in 8 of 8 captures (the counter over 5000 stimuli: mean 25.65, max 28.75 ns, none missed), the FPGA's share 3.75-5.0 ns pin to pin, the RF loop 17.5 ns; **the synchronous reply 72.5 ns** (counter mean 74.9), its FPGA share 51.25 ns; every reply right on the pins and as received (8 / 8 each); A2 the same 25.0 ns.  As LATENCY.md's board A (fast 23.3-25.3).  (6) **Across the boards, B -> A at 5200 MHz** (DSA 7.75, the leg 87 mV): prbs 0 errors in 2 Mbit for BPSK, on-off on IP and IN and the flip on IP; the Q bit 3e-4 at its natural place (frac 0) and Q on-off 3.6 % (the Q step on QP 39 mV against I's 85: open); A3 events right 98.3 / 100 / 9.8 / 95.9 % by message and A2 98.3 / 100 %, about 2000 false events a second at the idle; A1 and A1w (the I flip) only 12-15 % right with every event seen: the decisions involving the I flip are wrong, the Q steps right: open (the delay, settle and threshold sweeps are in the night's program).  (7) **Running from 17:38** (`twoboard.py night`, PID in `data/twoboard/night.pid`, records `data/twoboard/night_*.jsonl`): the prover on B then A (432 points each: 6 schemes x 2 paths x every reply function and each factor about its baseline), events (518 points a direction) and prbs (532) both ways at 5200 MHz, the band (22 frequencies 4400-6000, both ways, the loopback at each), events and prbs again at 5400 MHz (the WiFi gap) |
| 4.61 the prover swept on both boards, the received constellation, the paths, the nulls by frequency (2026-11-04 17:42-19:22 PST by the laptop's clock; the cabling as 4.59; Jason: C102 is lifted on board B too, as on A (4.44); 'sweep all parameters either randomly or systematically until tomorrow afternoon') | how fast and how consistently does each board turn a challenge round, and what moves it? | (1) **The windows from the received constellation** (twoboard ba2d7cc): the lock sits turned against the arms' middles (B's Q at the idle 19 mV off its no-carrier middle, 90 mV leg), so in a short event the I = -1 points land about 60 mV off in Q (A3's (-1, 1) reads QP 209 mV over the idle against (1, 1)'s 146): the centre-and-step windows fired QP / QN on every I flip (A1, A1w, A3's message 2, B1's (-1, 0)).  Now each scheme's points are measured as 200 ns events from its own idle on every comparator, and each window set across the gap between the points that must not fire it and those that must (frac the place in that gap).  A held point tells nothing (the loop re-centres it: Q the same at (1, 0) and (-1, 0) within 0.2 mV, and Q_OFF, I_OFF, Q_OFF_F +-0.15 V do not move it).  A pilot idle (0.2 of a point) is pulled round by the events themselves (B1's (1, 0) read on -I at 4 % duty): measured at 0.4 %.  The pilot found from near rho (30 mV of VR read nothing: the loop let go), both 45-degree signs tried.  (2) **The prover, loopback through each board's switch block, 5200 MHz** (`data/twoboard/night_20261104_17*.jsonl`, 18*; 432 points on B with the earlier windows, 304 on A and 160 more on B with the constellation's): A replies right in 696 of 716 captures, every scheme working.  The fast path RX ANT to TX ANT: A2 25.6 ns mean (max 26) on B whatever the delay, level (DSA 16-31.75), window place 0.15-0.6, hold or width, 24.5 on A; A3 25.1-30.0 ns by reply function on B (xor masks and the 9 derangements), 26.5-28.0 on A, single challenges 22.5-37.5 ns, the FPGA's share 3.75-15 ns by which comparator moves first and which ladder bits change; B1 24.6-26.1, B2 24.1 (max 26.2, no false event at any window place).  The synchronous reply 72-75 ns (A2 62 ns at decision delay 2-8, 51-53 ns with first-edge decisions on A2, B1, B2; A3's first edge decides wrong).  Window places 0.1-0.3 give means under 20 ns with 14-29 false events in 5000 and maxima of 35-42 ns: the early arrivals are false, not fast; A3 at 0.6 is 24.8 ns (max 28.8, 3 false).  The counter's minimum (13.75 ns every row) is the stimulus's own two-bit ladder change, not a reply.  A1 and A1w have one message, so no reply can differ from its stimulus: out of the prover.  (3) **Paths by the ripple** (`twoboard.py ripple`: the sender's carrier stepped +-50 MHz in 1 MHz, integer-N at a 1 MHz PFD, cp 15; the receiver's RX synthesizer fixed, its log detector; the FPV antenna on the DSA875): at A's receiver (B -> A) 2.1 dB p-p at 143 ns, the dish path's 139.75 against a direct one: B reaches A board to board about 18 dB under the link; at B's (A -> B) 3.0 dB p-p at 70 ns. The window placement is fixed in `scheme_windows` (a window no point fires at 2.499 V, one every point fires at 0) and checked at 06:50 (`flipq_20261106_064940`): with QP / QN at +849 mV A1 decided every event at all four levels, 45 650 / 45 653 / 46 244 / 48 925 of as many, the vector IN at the three lower legs and IP at 840 mV (IP / IN out of the DAC's range there, the table taking them as either).  The delay is left at 16 for now.  **The turn is B's transmitter's, and a VR_Q trim removes it:** with B's VR_Q offset from its null by -45 / -30 / -15 / 0 / +15 / +30 / +45 mV the plateau read -55 / -44 / -35 / -23 (-28 the second time) / -16 / -2 / +3 mV: 0.66 mV of Q arm per mV of VR_Q, zero at +38 mV; VR_I offset by +-30 mV left Q alone (-27 / -24; the leg 169 / 192). A pilot (the ladders at zero, VR offsets only) is unleaked while the points are leaked, so the points sit off the pilot's frame by the leak vector.  The trim is a calibration change (a 'driven null' per board and frequency beside the zero-ladder null): Jason's call.  **A's own transmitter, in loopback on A** (06:52, `flipq.py --loop A`, `flipq_20261106_065149`: the leg 59 / 59 / 76 / 98 mV at DSA 7.75 / 7.75 / 4 / 0, side +1 then -1 at DSA 0 with IP / IN dead there): the flip's Q plateau +31 / +35 / +39 / +52 % of the leg (less level-free than over the FPV pair: the loopback path is the switch block's leakage and the antennas' coupling, and the lock changed side at DSA 0); the trim 0.25 mV of Q arm per mV of VR_Q at a 66 mV leg, the same 0.0037 of the leg per mV as B's, zero near +70 mV. (REVB 66's calibration); whether the term is the same at every frequency and on both boards; what it does to the constellation and the decoder | **One VR_Q per board and frequency takes the quadrature out of the driven carrier, and it sits 32-49 mV above the zero-ladder null on both boards at every frequency.**  Fitted at two levels as Q = 2 x (the receiver's offset) + k(d) x leg with k linear in d: the sender's term at the null is 14-23 % of the leg (8-13 degrees; its sign follows the receiver's lock side), 0.0033-0.0047 of the leg per mV of VR_Q, zero at d* = +39.7 / +45.0 / +48.7 / +49.0 mV on A (VR_Q 0.3184 / 0.3170 / 0.3199 / 0.3210 V against nulls of 0.2787 / 0.2720 / 0.2712 / 0.2720) and +32.2 / +49.0 / +38.9 / +33.5 mV on B (0.3020 / 0.3197 / 0.2998 / 0.3116 against 0.2698 / 0.2707 / 0.2609 / 0.2781) at 4800 / 5000 / 5200 / 5400; A's first run at 4800 (21:02) gave +41.6.  The receivers' own Q offsets from the intercepts: A's +1.8 to +5.1 mV, B's -6.1 to +0.9 mV (B's trim holds).  The per-level zeros differ by up to 10 mV between the lock's leg and the 9 dB stronger one, so the two-level fit is the number.  At the driven null the flip's Q plateau reads 0.00-0.08 of the leg (0.11-0.21 at the null) and the -I corners' Q offset goes with it; A3 decodes 100 % at both settings with its window gaps within 20 mV of each other either way, so the decoder does not need it over cable (it is margin for the air and for any scheme that reads Q at the -I points).  The other points' asymmetries that remain (the (0, +-1) and (+-1, +-1) pairs 10-30 mV apart in Q or I at a 150-190 mV leg) are the Q ladder's own offset and the I/Q skew, not this term.  **A 200 ns carrier gap can put the loop on its other side:** the (0, 0) point from the full idle (the carrier off for 200 ns, 1-in-1000 for about 2 s: some 200 000 gaps) left the receiver's loop on the other side in 7 of the 16 constellation stages, on both receivers, and the (0, -1) point did it once (A -> B at 5400); neither the interference hold nor the signal holdover is enabled (RX_IHOLD_CFG 0x0006080a, RX_SHOLD_CFG 0xff1e400a, bit 0 clear on both boards), so it is the loop's own doing; counted gaps and a slow-timebase capture of the change follow in 4.84. |

The cause: `hold_point` wrote TX_IDLE as (1, 0) and TX_POINTS[0] as the held point, and in the hold mode the pins follow TX_POINTS[0]; `events_on` then wrote the points (message 0's point into TX_POINTS[0]) before switching the mode, so for the milliseconds between that write and TX_GO the carrier sat at the message's point: off for (0, 0), at +-90 degrees for the axis points, at the full point for a pilot scheme's idle; and the loop came back on whichever side it fell.
